  "short_description": {
    "id": "Štědrá bába adalah tokoh pembawa hadiah Natal dalam budaya rakyat Ceko, diperankan oleh orang yang menyamar dengan seprai putih dan topeng bangau.",
    "en": "Štědrá bába was a Christmas gift-bringing figure in Czech folk culture, played by a disguised person in a white sheet and a stork mask.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "stedra-baba-c01",
      "stedra-baba-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Štědrá bába, juga disebut vrtibába dan dijuluki klokavá, klovcová, atau šťuchává, adalah tokoh dalam budaya rakyat Ceko yang membagikan hadiah pada masa Natal. Ia termasuk kelompok tokoh pertengahan musim dingin yang disebut středozimky. Perannya dimainkan oleh perempuan, kadang laki-laki, yang menyamar dengan seprai putih dan topeng bangau. Selain membawa hadiah, ia berbuat lelucon dan mengancam dengan sapu ranting.",
      "en": "Štědrá bába, also called vrtibába and nicknamed klokavá, klovcová or šťuchává, was a figure of Czech folk culture who brought gifts at Christmas. She belonged to the midwinter figures known as středozimky. She was played by a woman, sometimes a man, disguised in a white sheet and a stork mask. Besides bringing gifts, she played pranks and threatened with a switch.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "stedra-baba-c01",
        "stedra-baba-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tokoh ini dikenal antara lain di Krkonoše dan Podkrkonoší, dan di Škodějov tradisinya masih hidup pada 1950-an. Menurut Čeněk Zíbrt, anak-anak di Vysoké nad Jizerou menggantungkan kaus kaki di dekat pintu pada malam Natal. Štědrá bába tidak pernah masuk ke ruangan sehingga tak terlihat; paginya anak-anak menemukan hadiah, sedangkan anak nakal bisa mendapat kentang beku. Sekitar tahun 2010 ada upaya menghidupkan kembali kebiasaan ini, misalnya di Tanvald.",
      "en": "She was known for example in Krkonoše and Podkrkonoší, and in Škodějov the tradition was still alive in the 1950s. According to Čeněk Zíbrt, children in Vysoké nad Jizerou hung stockings near the door on Christmas Eve. Štědrá bába never entered the room and so stayed unseen; in the morning the children found presents, while naughty ones might get frozen potatoes. Around 2010 there were attempts to revive the custom, for example in Tanvald.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "stedra-baba-c03",
        "stedra-baba-c04",
        "stedra-baba-c05"
      ]

      "locator": "úvod",

        "id": "Štědrá bába atau vrtibába, juga dijuluki klokavá, klovcová, atau šťuchává, adalah tokoh dalam budaya rakyat Ceko yang membagikan hadiah pada Natal dan tergolong \"středozimky\" (tokoh pertengahan musim dingin).",
        "en": "Štědrá bába or vrtibába, also nicknamed klokavá, klovcová or šťuchává, was a Christmas gift-bringing figure in Czech folk culture, one of the so-called středozimky (midwinter figures)."

    {
      "id": "stedra-baba-c02",
      "source_id": "stedra-baba-s1",
      "quote": "Byla představována převlečenou ženou, někdy i mužem, oděnou například v bílé prostěradlo a čapí masku. Kromě přinášení darů také prováděla různé žerty a hrozila metlou.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia diperankan oleh perempuan yang menyamar, kadang juga laki-laki, misalnya berbalut seprai putih dan bertopeng bangau; selain membawa hadiah, ia juga berbuat berbagai lelucon dan mengancam dengan sapu ranting.",

    },
    {
      "id": "stedra-baba-c03",
      "source_id": "stedra-baba-s1",
      "quote": "Známa byla například v Krkonoších a Podkrkonoší, ve Škodějově byla tato tradice živá ještě v 50. letech 20. století.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tokoh ini dikenal antara lain di Krkonoše dan Podkrkonoší; di Škodějov tradisinya masih hidup pada tahun 1950-an (dekade 50 abad ke-20).",
        "en": "She was known for example in the Krkonoše and Podkrkonoší regions; in Škodějov the tradition was still alive in the 1950s (the 50s of the 20th century)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stedra-baba-c04",
      "source_id": "stedra-baba-s1",
      "quote": "Podle Čeňka Zíbrta ve Vysokém nad Jizerou děti věší na Štědrý večer pro Štědrou bábu punčochy co nejblíže dveřím, ta nikdy nevstoupí do světnice a nelze ji tak spatřit, na Boží hod ráno v nich naleznou dárky, zlobivé děti ale mohou například dostat zmrzlé brambory.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Čeněk Zíbrt, di Vysoké nad Jizerou anak-anak menggantungkan kaus kaki untuk Štědrá bába sedekat mungkin dengan pintu pada malam Natal; ia tak pernah masuk ke ruangan sehingga tak bisa dilihat, dan pada pagi Hari Natal anak-anak menemukan hadiah di dalamnya, sedangkan anak nakal bisa mendapat kentang beku.",
        "en": "According to Čeněk Zíbrt, in Vysoké nad Jizerou children hang stockings for Štědrá bába as close to the door as possible on Christmas Eve; she never enters the room and so cannot be seen, and on Christmas Day morning they find presents in them, while naughty children may get frozen potatoes."
      }

    "en": "The sumpurnis is an evil being of Latvian mythology with a human body and a dog's head that lives in great forests and preys on people.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sumpurnis-c01",
      "sumpurnis-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Latvia, sumpurnis atau suņpurnis adalah makhluk jahat mirip manusia yang bertubuh manusia dan berkepala anjing. Dalam sebagian gambaran ia berkepala burung, bermata satu, atau berkaki satu. Ia konon tinggal di hutan besar dan berpakaian daun pohon.",
      "en": "In Latvian mythology the sumpurnis or suņpurnis is an evil humanlike being with a human body and a dog's head. Some descriptions give it a bird's head, one eye or one leg. It is said to live in great forests and to dress in tree leaves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sumpurnis-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ketika mengamuk, sumpurnis menyerang manusia, mencabik-cabiknya, memakan dagingnya, dan meminum darahnya. Korban yang tidak langsung dimakan diikat dan ditawan sampai tiba waktunya dimakan. Makhluk ini punya raja dan bangsawan yang ditandai ekor lebih panjang, dan kerabat terdekatnya adalah anjing.",
      "en": "When enraged, sumpurņi attack people, tear them apart, eat their flesh and drink their blood. Victims not eaten at once are tied up and held captive until it is time to eat them. These beings have a king and nobles marked by longer tails, and their closest relatives are dogs.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sumpurnis-c02",
        "sumpurnis-c03",
        "sumpurnis-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sumpurnis kadang dicampuradukkan dengan vilkacis, manusia serigala Latvia. Seperti vilkacis, orang bisa disihir menjadi sumpurnis oleh tukang sihir yang melingkarkan sabuk pada mereka; sihir itu patah bila sabuknya dilepas atau dirobek.",
      "en": "The sumpurnis is sometimes confused with the vilkacis, the Latvian werewolf. As with the vilkacis, people could be turned into sumpurņi by sorcerers who put a belt around them; the spell was broken when the belt was untied or torn.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sumpurnis-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Sifat sumpurnis kadang dipertentangan dengan manusia: ia pemakan daging manusia, pengembara atau pemburu, dan penutur bahasa yang tak dimengerti, sementara manusia memakan daging hewan, bertani, dan berbicara dengan bahasa yang dimengerti.",
    "en": "The sumpurnis is sometimes defined in opposition to humans: it eats human flesh, wanders or hunts, and speaks an unintelligible language, whereas people eat animal meat, farm and speak an intelligible one.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sumpurnis-c04"
    ]
  },

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sumpurnis-c01",
      "source_id": "sumpurnis-s1",
      "quote": "Sumpurnis, arī suņpurnis, latviešu mitoloģijā ir ļauna cilvēkveidīga būtne ar cilvēka rumpi un suņa galvu (dažkārt arī putna galvu, vienu aci vai vienu kāju), kas dzīvojot lielos mežos un tērpjoties ar koku lapām.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Latvia, sumpurnis atau suņpurnis adalah makhluk jahat mirip manusia bertubuh manusia dan berkepala anjing (kadang berkepala burung, bermata satu, atau berkaki satu) yang konon tinggal di hutan besar dan berpakaian daun pohon.",
        "en": "In Latvian mythology the sumpurnis or suņpurnis is an evil humanlike being with a human body and a dog's head (sometimes a bird's head, one eye or one leg), said to live in great forests and dress in tree leaves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sumpurnis-c02",
      "source_id": "sumpurnis-s1",
      "quote": "Satrakojušies sumpurņi uzbrūkot cilvēkiem, tos saplosot, apēdot to miesu un izdzerot asinis.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumpurnis yang mengamuk konon menyerang manusia, mencabik-cabiknya, memakan dagingnya, dan meminum darahnya.",
        "en": "Enraged sumpurņi are said to attack people, tear them apart, eat their flesh and drink their blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sumpurnis-c03",
      "source_id": "sumpurnis-s1",
      "quote": "Ja cilvēkus uzreiz neēduši, sumpurņi tos esot sasējuši un līdz ēšanai turējuši gūstā pie sevis.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jika tidak langsung dimakan, manusia konon diikat dan ditawan oleh sumpurnis sampai tiba waktunya dimakan.",
        "en": "If they did not eat people at once, the sumpurņi are said to have tied them up and kept them captive until eating them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sumpurnis-c04",
      "source_id": "sumpurnis-s1",
      "quote": "Dažkārt sumpurņi savās īpašībās pretnostatīti cilvēkiem, piemēram, kā cilvēku gaļas ēdāji pretstatā dzīvnieku gaļu ēdošajiem cilvēkiem, klejotāji/mednieki pretstatā cilvēkiem-zemkopjiem un nesaprotamā valodā runājošie pretstatā saprotamā valodā runājošajiem cilvēkiem.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sifat sumpurnis kadang dipertentangan dengan manusia: pemakan daging manusia lawan manusia pemakan daging hewan, pengembara/pemburu lawan petani, dan penutur bahasa yang tak dimengerti lawan manusia yang berbicara dengan bahasa yang dimengerti.",
        "en": "The sumpurņi are sometimes set in opposition to humans: eaters of human flesh versus people who eat animal meat, wanderers/hunters versus farmers, and speakers of an unintelligible language versus people speaking an intelligible one."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sumpurnis-c05",
      "source_id": "sumpurnis-s1",
      "quote": "Sumpurņiem bijis arī ķēniņš un citi sumpurņu augstdzimušie, kuru goda zīme bijusi pagarā aste, bet tuvākie sumpurņu radinieki bijuši suņi.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumpurnis juga punya raja dan bangsawan, yang tanda kehormatannya adalah ekor yang lebih panjang, sedangkan kerabat terdekat sumpurnis adalah anjing.",
        "en": "The sumpurņi also had a king and other nobles, whose mark of honour was a longer tail, and their closest relatives were dogs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sumpurnis-c06",
      "source_id": "sumpurnis-s1",
      "quote": "Dažkārt sumpurņi jaukti ar vilkačiem. Par sumpurņiem, tāpat kā vilkačiem, citus varēja noburt arī burvji, apjožot tiem apkārt jostu, kas burvestības atcelšanai pašiem vai kādam citam bijusi jāatraisa vai jāpārplēš.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumpurnis kadang dicampuradukkan dengan vilkacis (manusia serigala); seperti vilkacis, orang juga bisa disihir menjadi sumpurnis oleh tukang sihir yang melingkarkan sabuk pada mereka, dan untuk mematahkan sihir itu sabuknya harus dilepas atau dirobek oleh dirinya sendiri atau orang lain.",
        "en": "Sumpurņi were sometimes confused with vilkači (werewolves); like vilkači, people could be bewitched into sumpurņi by sorcerers who put a belt around them, and to break the spell the belt had to be untied or torn by the person or someone else."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sumpurnis-c07",
      "source_id": "sumpurnis-s1",
      "quote": "1929. un 2019. gadā ar nosaukumu „Sumpurņa pils” noticis Latvijas Mākslas akadēmijas karnevāls.",
      "locator": "ievads",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1929 dan 2019 Akademi Seni Latvia mengadakan karnaval bertajuk \"Sumpurņa pils\" (Istana Sumpurnis).",
        "en": "In 1929 and 2019 the Art Academy of Latvia held a carnival called \"Sumpurņa pils\" (The Sumpurnis Castle)."
      }
    }

    "id": "Talamaur adalah sosok vampir dalam legenda Kepulauan Banks, Vanuatu: orang hidup yang mengendalikan arwah orang mati untuk menguras daya hidup orang lain.",
    "en": "The talamaur is a vampire figure in the legends of the Banks Islands, Vanuatu: a living person who controls a dead person's ghost to drain the vitality of others.",

    {
      "id": "Talamaur adalah legenda vampir dari Kepulauan Banks, Vanuatu, di Pasifik Selatan. Talamaur mengendalikan arwah orang mati dan memakainya untuk menguras daya hidup orang hidup maupun orang yang baru meninggal. Sebagian orang bahkan ingin menjadi talamaur; kekuatannya konon didapat dengan memakan bagian jenazah, sehingga terjalin kekerabatan dengan jiwa orang yang meninggal itu.",
      "en": "The talamaur is a vampire legend of the Banks Islands, Vanuatu, in the South Pacific. A talamaur controls the ghost of a dead person and uses it to drain the vitality of the living and of the recently dead. Some people even aspired to become talamaurs; the power was supposedly gained by eating part of a corpse, creating kinship with the soul of the deceased.",

        "talamaur-c03"

    {

      "en": "A talamaur could be identified by the smoke of certain burning leaves, which made it shout the name of the dead person whose ghost it controlled and the name of its victim. Wounds dealt to its ghostly form showed on its living body, and such bruises served to identify it.",

        "en": "Both forms descend from Proto-Torres-Banks *tala-mauri, literally \"living soul\" (*tala \"soul\", *mauri \"to live\")."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Todorci adalah demon jahat setengah manusia setengah kuda dalam mitologi Slavia di Balkan, yang konon melintas pada Todorova subota menjelang akhir musim dingin.",
    "en": "The todorci are evil half-human, half-horse demons of Slavic mythology in the Balkans, said to ride on Todorova subota near the end of winter.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, todorci adalah makhluk setengah manusia dan setengah kuda. Mereka tampak seperti tumbuh dari punggung kuda, berjubah yang berkibar saat melaju menembus malam, dan wajah mereka tak pernah terlihat. Sebagai demon jahat, mereka termasuk makhluk mitos paling kuat dan kejam di Balkan, dan menginjak serta membunuh apa saja yang mereka temui di jalan dan permukiman.",
      "en": "In Slavic mythology the todorci are half human and half horse. They look as if grown from horses' backs, wear cloaks that flutter as they race through the night, and their faces are never seen. As evil demons they are among the most powerful and cruel mythical beings of the Balkans, trampling and killing whatever they meet on roads and in settlements.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "todorci-c01",
        "todorci-c02",
        "todorci-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Setahun sekali, menjelang akhir musim dingin pada hari yang disebut Todorova subota, derap kuku mereka kadang terdengar dari kejauhan setelah gelap. Pemimpin mereka, Veliki Todor, menunggang kuda putih, berselubung kain putih, pincang, dan membawa rantai yang bergemerincing.",
      "en": "Once a year, near the end of winter on the day called Todorova subota, their hoofbeats are sometimes heard from afar after dark. Their leader, Veliki Todor, rides a white horse, is wrapped in a white sheet, is lame and carries a rattling chain.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "todorci-c04",
        "todorci-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada hari itu perempuan memanggang kue berbentuk kuku kuda bernama todorčići atau kopitnjaci, dan sebagian kue wajib diberikan kepada kuda. Orang tidak bekerja dan anak-anak tidak boleh keluar rumah. Ritual ini, yang bertahan dalam perayaan Santo Todor, bertujuan menenangkan dan mengusir demon malam tersebut.",
      "en": "On that day women bake hoof-shaped cakes called todorčići or kopitnjaci, part of which must be given to the horses. No work is done and children may not leave the house. These rites, preserved in the feast of Saint Todor, aim to appease and drive away the night demons.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "todorci-c01",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Тодорци су били, у словенској митологији, пола људи, а пола коњи.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, todorci adalah makhluk setengah manusia, setengah kuda.",
        "en": "In Slavic mythology the todorci were half human and half horse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c02",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Они изгледају као да су израсли из коњских леђа, носе плаштове који се за њима вијоре док јуре кроз ноћ, и нико им никад није видео лице.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka tampak seperti tumbuh dari punggung kuda, mengenakan jubah yang berkibar saat mereka melaju menembus malam, dan tak seorang pun pernah melihat wajah mereka.",
        "en": "They look as if they had grown out of horses' backs, wear cloaks that flutter behind them as they race through the night, and no one has ever seen their faces."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c03",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Тодорци су зли демони и спадају у најмоћнија и најсуровија митска бића Балкана. Док пролазе путевима и насељима, они својим копитама изгазе и убијају све што им се нађе на путу.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Todorci adalah demon jahat dan termasuk makhluk mitos paling kuat dan paling kejam di Balkan; saat melintasi jalan dan permukiman, mereka menginjak dan membunuh apa saja yang menghalangi dengan kuku kudanya.",
        "en": "The todorci are evil demons and among the most powerful and cruel mythical beings of the Balkans; passing along roads and through settlements, they trample and kill everything in their way with their hooves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c04",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Једном годишње, негде пред крај зиме, на дан који се у народу зове Тодорова субота, кад се спусти мрак, понекад се из даљине чује топот копита од којих се укућанима леди крв у жилама.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setahun sekali, menjelang akhir musim dingin, pada hari yang oleh rakyat disebut Todorova subota (Sabtu Todor), saat gelap turun, kadang terdengar dari kejauhan derap kuku yang membuat penghuni rumah ngeri.",
        "en": "Once a year, near the end of winter, on the day popularly called Todorova subota (Theodore's Saturday), when darkness falls, the thunder of hooves is sometimes heard from afar, chilling the blood of the household."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c05",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Тодорци имају и свог вођу. Његово име је Велики Тодор. Од других тодораца се разликује по томе што јаше белог коња, огрнут је белим чаршавом, хром је, а у руци носи ланац којим звецка.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Todorci punya pemimpin bernama Veliki Todor (Todor Agung), yang berbeda dari yang lain karena menunggang kuda putih, berselubung kain putih, pincang, dan membawa rantai yang ia gemerincingkan.",
        "en": "The todorci have a leader named Veliki Todor (Great Todor), who differs from the others in riding a white horse, being wrapped in a white sheet, being lame and carrying a chain that he rattles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c06",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Чак и пре доласка Словена, на простору Балкана постојало је веровање везано за тајанствене ноћне коњанике.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bahkan sebelum kedatangan bangsa Slavia, di wilayah Balkan sudah ada kepercayaan tentang penunggang kuda malam yang misterius.",
        "en": "Even before the arrival of the Slavs, there was a belief in the Balkans about mysterious night horsemen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c07",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Пре Словена на простору Балкана живео је народ Трачана, који је веровао у Трачког коњаника, приносио му жртве и подизао му споменике.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebelum bangsa Slavia, di Balkan hidup bangsa Thrakia yang memercayai Penunggang Kuda Thrakia, mempersembahkan kurban kepadanya, dan mendirikan monumen untuknya.",
        "en": "Before the Slavs, the Balkans were home to the Thracians, who believed in the Thracian Horseman, made sacrifices to him and raised monuments to him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c08",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Неки обичаји у вези са њима су задржани у обредима везаним за славу Светог Тодора, који са демонским јахачима није имао никакве везе.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa adat tentang todorci bertahan dalam ritual perayaan Santo Todor (Theodore), meskipun santo itu sendiri tidak berhubungan dengan para penunggang demonik.",
        "en": "Some customs connected with them survive in rites for the feast of Saint Todor (Theodore), who himself had nothing to do with the demonic riders."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c09",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "На празник Тодорова субота, они се, наводно, појављују и на тај дан жене пеку нарочите колаче у облику коњских копита, који се зову тодорчићи или копитњаци. Део овог колача обавезно се даје коњима.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada hari raya Todorova subota mereka konon muncul, dan pada hari itu perempuan memanggang kue khusus berbentuk kuku kuda yang disebut todorčići atau kopitnjaci; sebagian kue itu wajib diberikan kepada kuda.",
        "en": "On the feast of Todorova subota they supposedly appear, and on that day women bake special cakes shaped like horse hooves called todorčići or kopitnjaci; part of the cake must be given to the horses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c10",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Обичај је да се на тај дан ништа не ради, а деци је забрањено да излазе из куће.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut adat, pada hari itu orang tidak bekerja dan anak-anak dilarang keluar rumah.",
        "en": "By custom no work is done on that day and children are forbidden to leave the house."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "todorci-c11",
      "source_id": "todorci-s1",
      "quote": "Ти обреди су свршени са циљем да се ови ноћни демони умилостиве и отерају.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ritual-ritual itu dilakukan untuk menenangkan dan mengusir demon malam ini.",
        "en": "These rites were performed to appease and drive away these night demons."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "Tur is a Slavic mythical creature from central Bosnia and Herzegovina. According to tales he is a giant bull holding the Earth on his back, like Kujata of Islamic mythology or the World Elephant. He is described as a giant black bull with curved, pointed horns.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ülemiste vanake adalah tokoh mitologis Estonia yang tinggal di Danau Ülemiste dekat Tallinn dan konon akan menenggelamkan kota itu bila sudah selesai dibangun.",
    "en": "The Ülemiste vanake is an Estonian mythological figure living in Lake Ülemiste near Tallinn, said to drown the city once it is finished.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ulemiste-vanake-c01",
      "ulemiste-vanake-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Estonia, Ülemiste vanake adalah tokoh mitologis yang tinggal di Danau Ülemiste dekat Tallinn. Ia konon ingin menenggelamkan Tallinn begitu kota itu selesai dibangun, sehingga ia harus selalu diberi tahu bahwa Tallinn belum selesai.",
      "en": "In Estonian folk religion the Ülemiste vanake is a mythological figure living in Lake Ülemiste near Tallinn. He is said to want to drown Tallinn as soon as it is finished, so he must always be told that Tallinn is not finished yet.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ulemiste-vanake-c01",
        "ulemiste-vanake-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebuah catatan cerita rakyat menyebut bahwa dahulu tidak ada danau di sana. Seorang lelaki yang sedang membajak tiba-tiba didatangi awan dan teriakan \"Minggir!\", lalu tenggelam dan menjadi penjaga danau. Pada malam Jaanilaupäev ia datang menanyakan apakah kota sudah selesai, dan jawabannya selalu belum.",
      "en": "A folklore record says there was once no lake there. A man ploughing the land was suddenly met by a cloud and a shout of \"Get away!\", then drowned and became the keeper of the lake. On Midsummer Eve he comes to ask whether the town is finished, and the answer is always no.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ulemiste-vanake-c03",
        "ulemiste-vanake-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

      "id": "ulemiste-vanake-c01",
      "source_id": "ulemiste-vanake-s1",
      "quote": "Ülemiste vanake on mütoloogiline tegelane eesti rahvausundis, kes pidavat elama Tallinna lähedal Ülemiste järves.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ülemiste vanake (Orang Tua Ülemiste) adalah tokoh mitologis dalam kepercayaan rakyat Estonia yang konon tinggal di Danau Ülemiste dekat Tallinn.",
        "en": "The Ülemiste vanake (Old Man of Ülemiste) is a mythological figure of Estonian folk religion said to live in Lake Ülemiste near Tallinn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulemiste-vanake-c02",
      "source_id": "ulemiste-vanake-s1",
      "quote": "Ta tahtvat Tallinna ära uputada, kui see valmis saab. Sellepärast peab talle alati ütlema, et Tallinn pole veel valmis.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon ia ingin menenggelamkan Tallinn begitu kota itu selesai dibangun; karena itu ia harus selalu diberi tahu bahwa Tallinn belum selesai.",
        "en": "He is said to want to drown Tallinn once it is finished; therefore he must always be told that Tallinn is not finished yet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulemiste-vanake-c03",
      "source_id": "ulemiste-vanake-s1",
      "quote": "Ülemiste järves on suur kivi. Enne polnud seal järve. Mees kündnud seal kohal, korraga pilv tuleb, karjutud: \"Eest ära!\" Uputanud mehe ära, mees jäänud järvevahiks.",
      "locator": "ERA II 114, 197/8 (2), 1935",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut catatan cerita rakyat, di Danau Ülemiste ada batu besar; dahulu di sana tidak ada danau. Seorang lelaki membajak di tempat itu, tiba-tiba datang awan dan terdengar teriakan \"Minggir!\"; lelaki itu tenggelam dan menjadi penjaga danau.",
        "en": "According to a folklore record, there is a big stone in Lake Ülemiste; formerly there was no lake. A man was ploughing there when suddenly a cloud came and someone shouted \"Get away!\"; the man was drowned and became the lake keeper."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulemiste-vanake-c04",
      "source_id": "ulemiste-vanake-s1",
      "quote": "Jaanilaupäeva öösel pidi käima küsimas, kas linn on valmis. Vastatud ikka, et ei ole valmis.",
      "locator": "ERA II 114, 197/8 (2), 1935",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada malam Jaanilaupäev (malam pertengahan musim panas), ia konon datang menanyakan apakah kota sudah selesai, dan selalu dijawab belum.",
        "en": "On Midsummer Eve (Jaanilaupäev) night he was said to come and ask whether the town was finished, and he was always told that it was not."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Monster Danau Vorota adalah kriptid yang dilaporkan terlihat di Danau Vorota, Siberia timur, terutama oleh dua ahli geologi Soviet pada 1953.",
    "en": "The Vorota beast is a cryptid reported from Lake Vorota in eastern Siberia, notably by two Soviet geologists in 1953.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vorota-beast-c01",
      "vorota-beast-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Monster Danau Vorota adalah monster danau yang dilaporkan terlihat di Danau Vorota, Siberia timur, Rusia. Danau ini salah satu dari beberapa danau di Dataran Tinggi Sordongnokh yang punya laporan penampakan serupa. Laporan dua ilmuwan Soviet tercatat dalam buku harian ekspedisi geologi Labynkyr yang disimpan di Akademi Ilmu Pengetahuan Rusia.",
      "en": "The Vorota beast is a lake monster reported from Lake Vorota in eastern Siberia, Russia. The lake is one of several on the Sordongnokh plateau with similar sighting reports. The account of two Soviet scientists is recorded in the diary of the Labynkyr geological expedition kept at the Russian Academy of Sciences.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vorota-beast-c01",
        "vorota-beast-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada 30 Juli 1953, ahli geologi Viktor A. Tverdokhlebov dan Boris Bashkatov sedang meneliti di tepi danau. Mereka melaporkan tubuh lonjong abu-abu kehitaman yang muncul sedikit di permukaan, dengan dua titik terang seperti mata dan benda seperti tongkat yang mencuat. Kepalanya diperkirakan selebar 6 kaki, tubuhnya 30 sampai 40 kaki, dan di punuknya ada sirip besar. Pada 1960-an beberapa ahli geologi lain juga mengaku melihat makhluk abu-abu raksasa bersirip.",
      "en": "On 30 July 1953 the geologists Viktor A. Tverdokhlebov and Boris Bashkatov were working by the lake. They reported an oblong grey-black body showing slightly above the surface, with two eye-like bright spots and a stick-like protrusion. Its head was estimated at about 6 feet wide, its body at 30 to 40 feet, with a large fin on its hump. In the 1960s other geologists also claimed to have seen a huge grey finned creature.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vorota-beast-c03",
        "vorota-beast-c04",
        "vorota-beast-c05",
        "vorota-beast-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": {

    {
      "id": "vorota-beast-c01",
      "source_id": "vorota-beast-s1",
      "quote": "沃罗塔湖水怪（英語：Vorota beast）是报导在俄罗斯西伯利亚东部的沃罗塔湖目击的湖怪。沃罗塔湖是索尔东诺赫（Сордонгнох/Sordongnokh）高原有湖怪目击报告的几个湖泊之一。",
      "locator": "序言",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Monster Danau Vorota (Vorota beast) adalah monster danau yang dilaporkan terlihat di Danau Vorota, Siberia timur, Rusia; danau itu salah satu dari beberapa danau di Dataran Tinggi Sordongnokh yang punya laporan penampakan monster.",
        "en": "The Vorota beast is a lake monster reported from Lake Vorota in eastern Siberia, Russia; the lake is one of several on the Sordongnokh plateau with monster sighting reports."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vorota-beast-c02",
      "source_id": "vorota-beast-s1",
      "quote": "保存于俄罗斯科学院的一份历史文献《苏联科学院东西伯利亚分院拉宾基尔(Labynkyr)地质勘探队日志》中记载了两位苏联科学家的目击报告。",
      "locator": "序言",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah dokumen sejarah yang disimpan di Akademi Ilmu Pengetahuan Rusia, yaitu buku harian ekspedisi geologi Labynkyr dari cabang Siberia Timur Akademi Ilmu Pengetahuan Uni Soviet, mencatat laporan penampakan dua ilmuwan Soviet.",
        "en": "A historical document kept at the Russian Academy of Sciences, the diary of the Labynkyr geological expedition of the East Siberian branch of the USSR Academy of Sciences, records the sighting report of two Soviet scientists."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vorota-beast-c03",
      "source_id": "vorota-beast-s1",
      "quote": "1953年7月30日，地质学家维克托·特维尔多赫列博夫（Viktor A. Tverdokhlebov）和鲍里斯·巴斯卡托夫（Boris Bashkatov）带领一个小队在湖边做地质研究。",
      "locator": "序言",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 30 Juli 1953, ahli geologi Viktor A. Tverdokhlebov dan Boris Bashkatov memimpin sebuah regu yang melakukan penelitian geologi di tepi danau.",
        "en": "On 30 July 1953 the geologists Viktor A. Tverdokhlebov and Boris Bashkatov led a small team doing geological research by the lake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vorota-beast-c04",
      "source_id": "vorota-beast-s1",
      "quote": "湖面上稍稍露出长圆形的灰黑胴体，上面能清楚看到两个像动物眼睛的对称亮点，从身体内还竖起一根类似棍子的东西。",
      "locator": "序言",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di permukaan danau tampak sedikit tubuh lonjong berwarna abu-abu kehitaman, dengan dua titik terang simetris seperti mata hewan dan sesuatu seperti tongkat yang mencuat dari tubuhnya.",
        "en": "An oblong grey-black body showed slightly above the surface, with two symmetrical bright spots like animal eyes and a stick-like object rising from the body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vorota-beast-c05",
      "source_id": "vorota-beast-s1",
      "quote": "它的头大约6英尺宽，身体大约30至40英尺长。在它的肉峰上有一个大的鳍。",
      "locator": "序言",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepalanya kira-kira selebar 6 kaki dan tubuhnya kira-kira 30 sampai 40 kaki panjangnya; di punuknya ada sirip besar.",
        "en": "Its head was about 6 feet wide and its body about 30 to 40 feet long; there was a large fin on its hump."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vorota-beast-c06",
      "source_id": "vorota-beast-s1",
      "quote": "在它的肉峰上有一个大的鳍。 20世纪60年代，有几个地质学家也声称看到了一个背上有鳍的巨大灰色生物。",
      "locator": "序言",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di punuknya ada sirip besar; pada 1960-an, beberapa ahli geologi juga mengaku melihat makhluk abu-abu raksasa bersirip di punggungnya.",
        "en": "There was a large fin on its hump; in the 1960s several geologists also claimed to have seen a huge grey creature with a fin on its back."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Vostrukha adalah roh rumah perempuan dalam tradisi Belarus yang bermata tajam, meskipun sebagian peneliti menganggap sosoknya rekaan etnograf abad ke-19, Pavel Shpilevsky.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Menurut ensiklopedia Brockhaus-Efron (ESBE), Vostrukha adalah nama Belarus untuk semacam roh rumah perempuan. Di rumah tempat ia tinggal tidak ada barang yang hilang, dan tak ada yang luput dari pandangannya yang tajam; di Kegubernuran Minsk orang memakai ungkapan \"melihat seperti Vostrukha\". Ada pula laporan tentang Hari Vostrukha yang diperingati pada 2 Maret beserta tanda dan adatnya.",
      "en": "According to the Brockhaus and Efron encyclopedia (ESBE), Vostrukha is the Belarusian name of some female house spirit. In a house where she lives nothing goes missing and nothing escapes her sharp eye; in Minsk Governorate people say \"we see as if Vostrukha\". There are also reports of a Vostrukha Day observed on 2 March, with its own omens and customs.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "vostrukha-c04"

      "en": "The figure became widely known through the Belarusian ethnographer P. M. Shpilevsky and the \"Dictionary of Slavic Mythology\", either as an analogue of the Russian domovoi or as an embodiment of the goddess Astraea guarding truth and chastity.       "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut E. E. Levkievskaya, penulis berbakat \"Belorusskie narodnye predaniya\" yang terbit pada pertengahan abad ke-19, Pavel Mikhailovich Shpilevsky (Drevlyansky), merekonstruksi sosok Vastrukha bersama tokoh-tokoh kreasi rakyat lainnya.",
        "en": "According to E. E. Levkievskaya, the talented author of the \"Belorusskie narodnye predaniya\" published in the mid-19th century, Pavel Mikhailovich Shpilevsky (Drevlyansky), recreated the figure of Vastrukha along with other folk characters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vostrukha-c07",
      "source_id": "vostrukha-s1",
      "quote": "Видимо, считает Левкиевская, Древлянский «сконструировал» названия для некоторых персонажей «по ассоциации с реальными словами: Ваструха — острый",
      "locator": "История",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Levkievskaya berpendapat Drevlyansky tampaknya \"merakit\" nama sebagian tokoh berdasarkan asosiasi dengan kata nyata, misalnya Vastrukha dari \"ostryi\" (tajam).",
        "en": "Levkievskaya thinks Drevlyansky apparently \"constructed\" names for some characters by association with real words, such as Vastrukha from \"ostryi\" (sharp)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vostrukha-c08",
      "source_id": "vostrukha-s1",
      "quote": "Так, например, появилась зоркая «Ваструха», белорусская богиня правды».",
      "locator": "История",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kritikus V. Kostyrko, dengan cara itulah muncul \"Vastrukha\" yang bermata tajam, dewi kebenaran Belarus.",
        "en": "According to the critic V. Kostyrko, this is how the sharp-eyed \"Vastrukha\", the Belarusian goddess of truth, came about."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vostrukha-c09",
      "source_id": "vostrukha-s1",
      "quote": "Мелкие седовласые старушки-вострухи — духи-защитницы дома",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam novel \"Molot Odina\" karya Aleksandr Prozorov, vostrukha digambarkan sebagai perempuan tua kecil berambut putih, roh pelindung rumah.",
        "en": "In Aleksandr Prozorov's novel \"Molot Odina\" the vostrukhi are small grey-haired old women, protective spirits of the house."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Keaslian tokoh",
        "en": "Authenticity of the figure"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "ESBE mencatatnya sebagai nama Belarus untuk roh rumah perempuan, lengkap dengan ungkapan rakyat.",
            "en": "The ESBE records it as a Belarusian name for a female house spirit, with a folk saying."
          },

          ]
        },

          },

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vuokko adalah makhluk pembantu dukun dalam mitologi Sami yang menjalankan urusan sang dukun dan membawa jiwanya saat trans.",
    "en": "The vuokko is a shaman's helper creature in Sami mythology that runs errands for the shaman and carries the shaman's soul in trance.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Sami, vuokko adalah makhluk pembantu seorang dukun (noita). Ia menjalankan urusan atas nama dukun itu, termasuk mendatangkan kekayaan dengan mencurinya dari orang lain. Vuokko juga bisa membawa jiwa sang dukun dalam perjalanan saat keadaan trans.",
      "en": "In Sami mythology the vuokko is the helper creature of a shaman (noita). It runs errands on the shaman's behalf, including bringing wealth by stealing it from others. The vuokko could also carry the shaman's soul on its journey during a trance.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vuokko-c01",
        "vuokko-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam satu cerita lain, vuokko justru berupa burung raksasa yang mengintai kapal-kapal yang lewat dari sebuah pulau batu. Namanya merupakan kata pinjaman dari kata yang berarti burung raksasa mitologis (vaakalintu).",
      "en": "In another story, however, the vuokko is a gigantic bird lurking on a rocky island for passing ships. Its name is a loan from a word meaning a giant mythical bird (vaakalintu).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vuokko-c03",
        "vuokko-c04"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vyal adalah cmok (naga) dalam legenda Belarus dari desa Valeikishki yang mencuri ternak dan dibunuh dengan domba berisi ter panas dan belerang.",
    "en": "Vyal is a tsmok (dragon) of Belarusian legend from the village of Valeikishki who stole cattle and was killed with a ram stuffed with hot tar and sulphur.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vyal-c01",
      "vyal-c02",
      "vyal-c03",
      "vyal-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Vyal adalah salah satu cmok atau naga. Legendanya dicatat oleh Iryna Dubianetskaya di desa Valeikishki, distrik Astravets. Vyal tinggal di dekat sebuah batu besar, bermain-main dan melemparkan batu itu, menghalangi orang lewat, dan mencuri ternak penduduk.",
      "en": "In Belarusian mythology Vyal is one of the tsmoki, or dragons. His legend was recorded by Iryna Dubianetskaya in the village of Valeikishki, Astravets district. Vyal lived by a big boulder, played with it and tossed it about, blocked people's way and stole the villagers' cattle.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vyal-c01",
        "vyal-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seorang penduduk desa yang cerdik mengisi kulit domba jantan dengan ter dan belerang, lalu menaruhnya di dekat batu. Vyal menelannya, ter panas memenuhi tenggorokannya, dan ia mati. Raungan terakhirnya begitu keras sehingga cmok Krok yang tinggal dekat Kraków ikut mati ketakutan.",
      "en": "A clever villager filled a ram's skin with tar and sulphur and set it by the stone. Vyal swallowed it, the hot tar filled his throat and he died. His last roar was so loud that the tsmok Krok, living near Kraków, died of fright.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vyal-c03",
        "vyal-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Vyal dikaitkan dengan dewa-dewa Balt pelindung orang mati dan dunia bawah, seperti Vels dan Velinas, serta dengan Veles. Cara kematiannya sejajar dengan legenda Polandia tentang Naga Wawel di dekat Kraków.",
      "en": "The name Vyal is linked with Baltic gods of the dead and the underworld such as Vels and Velinas, and with Veles. The manner of his death parallels the legend of the Wawel Dragon near Kraków in Poland.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vyal-c05",
        "vyal-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

      "id": "vyal-c01",
      "source_id": "vyal-s1",
      "quote": "Вяль — у беларускай міфалогіі адзін з цмокаў. Паданне аб ім запісана Ірынай Дубянецкай у в. Валэйкішкі (Астравецкі раён).",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Vyal adalah salah satu cmok (naga); legendanya dicatat oleh Iryna Dubianetskaya di desa Valeikishki (distrik Astravets).",
        "en": "In Belarusian mythology Vyal is one of the tsmoki (dragons); his legend was recorded by Iryna Dubianetskaya in the village of Valeikishki (Astravets district)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vyal-c02",
      "source_id": "vyal-s1",
      "quote": "Вяль жыў каля валуна бліз вёскі Валэйкішкі, бавіўся з ім, перакідваў і не даваў людзям прайсці, а таксама краў у іх скаціну.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vyal tinggal di dekat sebuah batu besar dekat desa Valeikishki, bermain-main dengan batu itu, melemparkannya, menghalangi orang lewat, dan mencuri ternak mereka.",
        "en": "Vyal lived by a boulder near the village of Valeikishki, played with it, tossed it about, kept people from passing and stole their cattle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vyal-c03",
      "source_id": "vyal-s1",
      "quote": "Аднойчы разумны вясковец закалоў барана, злупіў з яго скуру, заліў у яе «смалу і серку» і паставіў каля камня. Цмок убачыў «барана» і адразу з’еў.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suatu hari seorang penduduk desa yang cerdik menyembelih seekor domba jantan, menguliti dan mengisi kulitnya dengan \"ter dan belerang\", lalu menaruhnya di dekat batu; sang cmok melihat \"domba\" itu dan langsung memakannya.",
        "en": "One day a clever villager slaughtered a ram, skinned it, filled the skin with \"tar and sulphur\" and set it by the stone; the tsmok saw the \"ram\" and ate it at once."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vyal-c04",
      "source_id": "vyal-s1",
      "quote": "Гарачая смала заліла яму глотку, і ён адразу памёр. Перад смерцю Вяль зароў так громка, што яго пачуў цмок Крок, што жыў пад Кракавам, і сам адразу памёр са страху.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ter panas memenuhi tenggorokannya dan ia langsung mati; sebelum mati Vyal meraung begitu keras sehingga terdengar oleh cmok Krok yang tinggal dekat Kraków, yang ikut mati ketakutan.",
        "en": "Hot tar filled his throat and he died at once; before dying Vyal roared so loudly that the tsmok Krok, who lived near Kraków, heard him and himself died of fright."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vyal-c05",
      "source_id": "vyal-s1",
      "quote": "Імя Вяль адсылае да імёнаў балцкіх багоў-апекуноў памерлых і падзем’я: Vels у латышоў, Velinas у літоўцаў, а таксама Вялеса, імя якога генетычна да іх узыходзіць.",
      "locator": "Паралелі ў іншых легендах",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Vyal merujuk pada nama dewa-dewa Balt pelindung orang mati dan dunia bawah: Vels pada orang Latvia, Velinas pada orang Lituania, serta Veles yang namanya berasal-usul sama.",
        "en": "The name Vyal points to the names of Baltic gods who guard the dead and the underworld: Vels among the Latvians, Velinas among the Lithuanians, and also Veles, whose name is genetically related."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vyal-c06",
      "source_id": "vyal-s1",
      "quote": "У Польшчы захавалася легенда пра Вавельскага цмока, які жыў у прыгорку пад Кракавам і быў знішчаны гэтакім жа чынам, як і Вяль",
      "locator": "Паралелі ў іншых легендах",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Polandia ada legenda tentang Naga Wawel yang tinggal di bukit dekat Kraków dan dibinasakan dengan cara yang sama seperti Vyal.",
        "en": "In Poland there is a legend of the Wawel Dragon, who lived in a hill near Kraków and was destroyed in the same way as Vyal."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

      "searched": "Item Wikidata Q17047240 hanya punya artikel Wikipedia be. Artikel Iryna Dubianetskaya yang dirujuk hanya tersedia sebagai arsip dan belum dibuka."
    },

    }
  ]
}

      "id": "Wars dan Sawa adalah tokoh dalam mitos asal-usul pendirian dan nama kota Warsawa, ibu kota Polandia. Legenda kemunculan mereka punya beberapa versi. Dalam salah satunya, adipati atau raja Kazimierz, yang kadang disamakan dengan Siemowit I dari Masovia, tersesat saat berburu di hutan yang kini menjadi Kota Tua Warsawa.",
      "en": "Wars and Sawa are characters in the origin myth of the founding and name of Warsaw, capital of Poland. The legend exists in several versions. In one, the duke or king Kazimierz, sometimes identified with Siemowit I of Masovia, got lost while hunting in the forest that is now Warsaw Old Town.",

        "wars-and-sawa-c01",
        "wars-and-sawa-c02"

    {
      "id": "Ia singgah di pondok seorang perempuan yang baru melahirkan bayi kembar dan dijamu dengan murah hati.       "en": "He stopped at the hut of a woman who had just given birth to twins and was hosted generously. He named the twins Wars and Sawa and gave her money for a bigger house. The settlement that grew around it was named Warsawa, which became Warszawa.",

    {

      "id": "wars-and-sawa-c03",
      "source_id": "wars-and-sawa-s1",
      "quote": "He came across a small adobe hut, inhabited by a woman who had recently given birth to twins. The woman hosted him very generously. He named her two children Wars and Sawa and gave the woman money to build a bigger house.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "He came upon a small adobe hut where a woman had just given birth to twins; she hosted him generously, and he named the children Wars and Sawa and gave her money to build a bigger house."

    {
      "id": "wars-and-sawa-c04",
      "source_id": "wars-and-sawa-s1",
      "quote": "Later, other people moved near the house, forming a settlement, which was named after the twins, Warsawa, which later evolved to Warszawa, the name of the city of Warsaw in the Polish language.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      "source_id": "wars-and-sawa-s1",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kasny adalah roh jahat dalam tradisi Belarus yang menyerang berkelompok dan mengisap korban seperti lintah sampai mati.",
    "en": "The kasny are evil spirits of Belarusian tradition that attack in groups and suck on their victims like leeches until they die.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q114458257-c01",
      "wd-q114458257-c02",
      "wd-q114458257-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kasny adalah roh jahat yang bertindak berkelompok. Setelah menyerang, mereka menempel pada korban dan mengisapnya seperti lintah sampai korban mati. Makin korban melawan, makin kuat mereka melekat. Satu kasny saja tidak berbahaya, tetapi bila dua atau tiga menyerang, yang lain bisa memanggil kawanan tambahan.",
      "en": "The kasny are evil spirits that act in groups. Once they attack, they cling to a victim and suck on it like leeches until it dies. The more the victim resists, the harder they cling. A single kasny is harmless, but when two or three attack, the others can call in more.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q114458257-c01",
        "wd-q114458257-c03",
        "wd-q114458257-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Cicitan mereka hanya bisa didengar korban yang membawa \"garam Kamis\", garam yang diberkati di gereja pada Kamis Putih. Kasny berbagi ciri dengan makhluk pengisap darah lain seperti vupyr dan eretnik. Namanya mungkin berkaitan dengan kata dialek Belarus dan Rusia yang berarti buruk atau najis.",
      "en": "Their squeaking can be heard only by a victim carrying \"Thursday salt\", salt blessed in church on Maundy Thursday. The kasny share traits with other bloodsuckers such as the vupyr and eretnik. Their name may be related to Belarusian and Russian dialect words meaning bad or unclean.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q114458257-c02",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q114458257-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q114458257-s1",
      "quote": "Кáсны — ліхія духі, якія дзейнічаюць гуртам.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kasny adalah roh jahat yang bertindak berkelompok.",
        "en": "The kasny are evil spirits that act in groups."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114458257-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q114458257-s1",
      "quote": "Назва касны магчыма звязана з беларускім дыялектным касны «сараматны, дрэнны, худы», рускім дыялектным кáстный, кастнóй «паскудны, гідкі, брыдкі, нячысты»",
      "locator": "Этымалогія імя",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama kasny mungkin berkaitan dengan kata dialek Belarus \"kasny\" (memalukan, buruk, kurus) dan kata dialek Rusia \"kastnyi, kastnoi\" (keji, menjijikkan, jelek, najis).",
        "en": "The name kasny may be related to the Belarusian dialect word kasny \"shameful, bad, thin\" and the Russian dialect kastnyi, kastnoi \"vile, disgusting, ugly, unclean\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114458257-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q114458257-s1",
      "quote": "Напаўшы на ахвяру, прысмоктваюцца да яе, нібы п’яўкі, пакуль тая не памрэ. Чым больш ахвяра супраціўляецца, тым з большай сілаю яны прыстаюць да яе, нібы ахопленыя злобай.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah menyerang korban, mereka menempel dan mengisapnya seperti lintah sampai korban mati; makin korban melawan, makin kuat mereka melekat, seolah dikuasai amarah.",
        "en": "Having attacked a victim, they suck onto it like leeches until it dies; the more the victim resists, the harder they cling, as if seized by malice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114458257-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q114458257-s1",
      "quote": "Калі прыстае адзін з каснаў, то баяцца няма чаго. Калі ж напад зроблены двума-трыма каснамі, то пакуль адзін ля ахвяры, астатнія могуць склікаць іншых.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jika hanya satu kasny yang menempel, tidak ada yang perlu ditakuti; tetapi jika dua atau tiga menyerang, selagi satu di dekat korban, yang lain bisa memanggil kawan-kawannya.",
        "en": "If only one kasny clings there is nothing to fear; but if two or three attack, while one stays by the victim the others can summon more."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114458257-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q114458257-s1",
      "quote": "Іх піск ахвяра можа пачуць толькі маючы пры сабе такі абярэг як «чацвярговая соль» — гэта значыць соль, асвечаная ў храме на Чысты чацвер.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korban hanya bisa mendengar cicitan mereka bila membawa jimat \"garam Kamis\", yaitu garam yang diberkati di gereja pada Kamis Putih.",
        "en": "A victim can hear their squeaking only when carrying the amulet called \"Thursday salt\", salt blessed in church on Maundy Thursday."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114458257-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q114458257-s1",
      "quote": "Гэтыя нячысцікі маюць агульныя рысы з іншымі крывасмокамі: вупырамі, ератнікамі.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk najis ini punya ciri yang sama dengan pengisap darah lain seperti vupyr dan eretnik.",
        "en": "These unclean beings share traits with other bloodsuckers such as the vupyr and eretnik."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hartsuki adalah roh-roh hitam pelayan Perun yang hanya dikenal dari tulisan etnograf Belarus Pavel Shpilevsky dan kemungkinan besar karangannya sendiri.",
    "en": "The hartsuki are black spirits serving Perun, known only from the writings of the Belarusian ethnographer Pavel Shpilevsky and probably his own invention.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q114728962-c01",
      "wd-q114728962-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hartsuki adalah tokoh yang diajukan oleh P. Shpilevsky sebagai roh-roh hitam yang tunduk kepada Perun. Menurut gambarannya, mereka tinggal di gunung besar, bermain seperti anak-anak saat fajar dan senja, berkuda melintasi ladang dan hutan pada siang hari, dan pada malam hari terbang di udara dalam wujud burung pemangsa atas perintah Perun. Mereka juga menarik batu gilingan guntur yang ditunggangi Perun dan menimbulkan angin serta badai.",
      "en": "The hartsuki are characters proposed by P. Shpilevsky as black spirits subject to Perun. In his description they live in great mountains, play like children at dawn and dusk, ride across fields and forests at noon, and at night fly through the air as birds of prey at Perun's command. They also haul the thunder millstone on which Perun rides and raise winds and storms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q114728962-c01",
        "wd-q114728962-c02",
        "wd-q114728962-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tokoh-tokoh ini hanya muncul dalam teks Pavel Shpilevsky. Peneliti kemudian mencatat bahwa petani Belarus tidak mengenal mereka dan bahwa Shpilevsky, yang dikenal banyak memalsukan mitos dan cerita rakyat, mungkin mengarangnya.",
      "en": "These characters appear only in Pavel Shpilevsky's texts. Later researchers noted that Belarusian peasants did not know them and that Shpilevsky, known for many falsifications of myth and folklore, may have invented them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q114728962-c04",
        "wd-q114728962-c05"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q114728962-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q114728962-s1",
      "quote": "Гарцукі — прапанаваныя П. Шпілеўскім персанажы, чорныя духі, падначаленыя Перуну. Іх імя паходзіць ад слова «гарцаваць» — гуляць, бегаць навыперадкі.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hartsuki adalah tokoh yang diajukan oleh P. Shpilevsky: roh-roh hitam yang tunduk kepada Perun; namanya berasal dari kata \"hartsavats\" (bermain, berlari berlomba).",
        "en": "The hartsuki are characters proposed by P. Shpilevsky: black spirits subject to Perun; their name comes from the word \"hartsavats\" (to frolic, to race)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114728962-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q114728962-s1",
      "quote": "Жывуць яны ў вялікіх гарах, дзе падчас усходу і заходу сонца гуляюць, нібы дзеці. Апоўдні стралою носяцца на конях па палях і лясах, а ўначы па даручэнні Перуна лятаюць у паветры, набываючы аблічча розных драпежных птушак.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut gambaran itu, mereka tinggal di gunung-gunung besar dan bermain seperti anak-anak saat matahari terbit dan terbenam; siang hari mereka melesat berkuda melintasi ladang dan hutan, dan malam hari atas perintah Perun terbang di udara dalam wujud berbagai burung pemangsa.",
        "en": "In this description they live in great mountains and play like children at sunrise and sunset; at noon they dash on horseback across fields and forests, and at night, on Perun's orders, fly through the air in the form of various birds of prey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114728962-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q114728962-s1",
      "quote": "Гарцукі возяць па небе грамавы жоран, на якім раз’язджае Пярун з вогненным лукам; яны ўтвараюць вятры, буру і віхры і іншыя стыхійныя прыродныя з’явы.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hartsuki menarik batu gilingan guntur melintasi langit, yang ditunggangi Perun dengan busur berapi; mereka menimbulkan angin, badai, pusaran angin, dan gejala alam lainnya.",
        "en": "The hartsuki haul the thunder millstone across the sky, on which Perun rides with a fiery bow; they raise winds, storms, whirlwinds and other natural phenomena."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114728962-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q114728962-s1",
      "quote": "Персанажы згаданы толькі ў тэктах этнографа Паўла Шпілеўскага. Пазнейшыя даследчыкі заўважалі, што беларускія сяляне нічога не чулі пра гэтых персанажаў і што Шпілеўскі, магчыма, проста іх выдумаў.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tokoh-tokoh ini hanya disebut dalam teks etnograf Pavel Shpilevsky; peneliti kemudian mencatat bahwa petani Belarus tidak pernah mendengar tentang mereka dan bahwa Shpilevsky mungkin hanya mengarangnya.",
        "en": "These characters are mentioned only in the texts of the ethnographer Pavel Shpilevsky; later researchers noted that Belarusian peasants had heard nothing of them and that Shpilevsky may simply have invented them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q114728962-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q114728962-s1",
      "quote": "Павел Шпілеўскі вядомы шматлікімі фальсіфікацыямі міфаў і фальклора.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pavel Shpilevsky dikenal karena banyak memalsukan mitos dan cerita rakyat.",
        "en": "Pavel Shpilevsky is known for numerous falsifications of myths and folklore."
      }
    }

}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Banteng Hujan adalah tokoh dalam beberapa mitos orang San (Bushmen) yang bertugas membawa hujan ke bumi; ke mana pun ia pergi, hujan turun.",
    "en": "The Rain Bull is a figure in some San (Bushman) myths whose duty is to bring rain to the earth; wherever he goes, rain falls.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q12279265-c01",
      "wd-q12279265-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam beberapa mitos orang San (Bushmen), Banteng Hujan adalah tokoh sampingan yang tugas utamanya membawa hujan ke bumi, tugas yang ia jalankan dengan gembira. Ia tinggal di langit tetapi kadang turun ke tengah manusia dan biasanya berdiam di danau. Ke mana pun ia pergi, di sana turun hujan, sehingga orang San yang melihatnya mengikutinya untuk menemukan tanah yang kaya akar manis dan hewan buruan.",
      "en": "In some San (Bushman) myths the Rain Bull is an episodic figure whose main task, carried out with joy, is to bring rain to the earth. He lives in the sky but sometimes comes down among people, usually dwelling in lakes. Wherever he goes rain falls, so San people who see him follow him to find land rich in sweet roots and game.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12279265-c01",
        "wd-q12279265-c02",
        "wd-q12279265-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitos Kua dan Banteng Hujan, hujan turun ke bumi dalam wujud banteng dan tiba di sebuah pondok tempat Kua terbaring sakit parah, sementara saudarinya menaburkan daun buchu di sekitarnya. Tergerak oleh iba, Banteng Hujan menyanyikan lagu penyembuh ajaib, dan Kua pun sembuh. Kua naik ke punggungnya dan menaburkan buchu di kepalanya, lalu mereka pergi bersama. Akhirnya, di sebuah mata air, Banteng Hujan menemukan rumahnya, tempat ia mengirim hujan pemberi hidup bagi semua makhluk.",
      "en": "In the myth of Kua and the Rain Bull, the rain comes down to earth as a bull and reaches a hut where Kua lies gravely ill while her sister scatters buchu leaves around her. Moved by pity, the Rain Bull sings a magic healing song and Kua recovers. Kua climbs onto his back and sprinkles buchu on his head, and they travel together. In the end, at a spring, the Rain Bull finds his home, from which he sends life-giving rain to all creatures.",

  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "url": "https://bg.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%94%D1%8A%D0%B6%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B5%D0%BD_%D0%B1%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%BE%D0%BB",
      "title": "Дъждовен бивол",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (bg)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "bg",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Дъждовен бивол е епизодичен персонаж в някои бушменски митове. Основното му задължение е да носи дъжд на земята. Изпълнява тази задача с удоволствие и радост.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banteng Hujan (bg: Dazhdoven bivol) adalah tokoh sampingan dalam beberapa mitos orang San (Bushmen); tugas utamanya membawa hujan ke bumi, dan ia melakukannya dengan senang hati.",
        "en": "The Rain Bull (bg: Dazhdoven bivol) is an episodic character in some San (Bushman) myths; his main duty is to bring rain to the earth, which he does with pleasure and joy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Той живее на небето, но понякога слиза и при хората. Обикновено живее в езерата, а когато се запъти нанякъде, там, накъдето се е отправил, завалява дъжд.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tinggal di langit tetapi kadang turun ke tengah manusia; biasanya ia tinggal di danau, dan ke mana pun ia pergi, di sana turun hujan.",
        "en": "He lives in the sky but sometimes comes down among people; he usually dwells in lakes, and wherever he heads, rain falls there."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Ако го видят, бушмените го следват с целия си багаж за да намерят земя, богата на сладки корени и дивеч.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jika melihatnya, orang San mengikutinya dengan seluruh barang bawaan untuk menemukan tanah yang kaya akar manis dan hewan buruan.",
        "en": "If they see him, the San follow him with all their belongings to find land rich in sweet roots and game."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Веднъж дъждът слязъл на земята приемайки формата на Дъждовния бик.",
      "locator": "Митът за Куа и Дъждовния бик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitos Kua dan Banteng Hujan, suatu kali hujan turun ke bumi dalam wujud Banteng Hujan.",
        "en": "In the myth of Kua and the Rain Bull, the rain once came down to earth in the form of the Rain Bull."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Това била Куа, която лежала тежко болна, а край нея се въртяла сестра ѝ и се опитвала да ѝ помогне.",
      "locator": "Митът за Куа и Дъждовния бик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menemukan Kua, perempuan muda yang terbaring sakit parah, sementara saudarinya berusaha menolongnya.",
        "en": "He found Kua, a young woman lying gravely ill, while her sister tried to help her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Сестрата разтривала листа от билката бучу (Agathosma betulina) и ги разпръсквала около тялото на Куа, опитвайки се да я излекува.",
      "locator": "Митът за Куа и Дъждовния бик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saudarinya menggosok daun herba buchu (Agathosma betulina) dan menaburkannya di sekitar tubuh Kua untuk menyembuhkannya.",
        "en": "The sister rubbed leaves of the herb buchu (Agathosma betulina) and scattered them around Kua's body to heal her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Гледката на младата страдаща жена изпълнило сърцето на Дъждовния бик със съчувствие и болка. Той тихичко започнал да пее една от вълшебните си целебни песни.",
      "locator": "Митът за Куа и Дъждовния бик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Melihat perempuan muda yang menderita itu, hati Banteng Hujan dipenuhi iba, dan ia mulai menyanyikan pelan salah satu lagu penyembuh ajaibnya.",
        "en": "The sight of the suffering young woman filled the Rain Bull's heart with pity, and he quietly began to sing one of his magic healing songs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c08",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Постепенно болестта започнала да я напуска, тя се изправила, взела детето от ръцете на сестра си и го залюляла.",
      "locator": "Митът за Куа и Дъждовния бик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perlahan penyakitnya hilang; Kua bangkit, mengambil bayinya dari tangan saudarinya, dan menimangnya.",
        "en": "Gradually the illness left her; she rose, took her child from her sister's arms and rocked it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c09",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Разбрал, че е намерил своя дом, откъдето да изпраща животворен дъжд по цялата земя за всички живи създания.",
      "locator": "Митът за Куа и Дъждовния бик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akhirnya di sebuah mata air jernih ia sadar telah menemukan rumahnya, tempat ia mengirim hujan pemberi hidup ke seluruh bumi bagi semua makhluk.",
        "en": "Finally, at a clear spring, he realised he had found his home, from which to send life-giving rain over all the earth for every living creature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12279265-c10",
      "source_id": "wd-q12279265-s1",
      "quote": "Качила се на гърба му и разпръснала част от билките върху главата му. От всичко това той се почувствал щастлив и въодушевен, обърнал се и потеглил в тръс с Куа на гърба си.",
      "locator": "Митът за Куа и Дъждовния бик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kua naik ke punggungnya dan menaburkan sebagian herba ke kepalanya; sang banteng merasa bahagia dan bersemangat, lalu berlari kecil membawa Kua di punggungnya.",
        "en": "Kua climbed onto his back and scattered some of the herbs on his head; he felt happy and elated and trotted off with Kua on his back."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "culture",
      "searched": "Petunjuk batch menempatkannya di kelompok Slavia/Eropa Timur, tetapi sumber menyebutnya tokoh mitos orang San (Bushmen); dipakai tradition-southern-african/africa."
    },
    {

    },
    {

    },
    {

    "id": "Kenchrines adalah ular raksasa mitos, mungkin berkepala singa, dari pulau-pulau Thrakia di Laut Aegea yang disebut oleh Nikandros.",
    "en": "The kenchrines is a mythical giant snake, perhaps lion-headed, of the Thracian islands of the Aegean mentioned by Nicander.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kenchrines adalah monster mitos yang menghuni pulau-pulau Thrakia di Laut Aegea dan disebut oleh penulis Nikandros. Ia juga dijuluki \"singa belang\" atau \"singa bertotol sisik\", dan digambarkan sebagai ular raksasa yang kemungkinan berkepala singa.",
      "en": "The kenchrines is a mythical monster of the Thracian islands of the Aegean Sea, mentioned by the writer Nicander. Also called the \"spotted lion\" or \"lion speckled with scales\", it is described as a huge snake, probably lion-headed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12282902-c01",
        "wd-q12282902-c02"

    },
    {
      "id": "Ia membunuh dengan racun kuat yang membusukkan kulit, menggerogoti sendi, dan menimbulkan sakit perut hebat, atau dengan melilit, mencekik, dan mengisap darah korbannya. Ia menyerang pada tengah hari, biasanya memangsa domba yang ditinggal penggembala, tetapi juga mematikan bagi manusia.",
      "en": "It kills with a strong venom that rots the skin, eats away the joints and causes severe stomach pain, or by coiling round, strangling and sucking the blood of its victim. It attacks at noon, usually preying on sheep left unattended by shepherds, but is deadly to humans too.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12282902-c03",
        "wd-q12282902-c04"
      ]
    }

      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q12282902-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q12282902-s1",
      "quote": "Кенхринес (kenchrines) – митично чудовище, обитаващо тракийските острови в Егейско море, споменато от Никандър.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kenchrines adalah monster mitos yang menghuni pulau-pulau Thrakia di Laut Aegea, disebut oleh Nikandros (Nicander).",
        "en": "The kenchrines is a mythical monster inhabiting the Thracian islands of the Aegean Sea, mentioned by Nicander."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12282902-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q12282902-s1",
      "quote": "Наричано още „шарен лъв“ (или „пропъстрен с люспи лъв“), то представлява огромна змия, вероятно с лъвска глава.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga disebut \"singa belang\" (atau \"singa bertotol sisik\") dan berupa ular raksasa, kemungkinan berkepala singa.",
        "en": "Also called the \"spotted lion\" (or \"lion speckled with scales\"), it is a huge snake, probably with a lion's head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12282902-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q12282902-s1",
      "quote": "Кенхринес убива със силна отрова, причиняваща загнивания на кожата, разяждане на ставите и силни стомашни болки или обвивайки и задушавайки жертвата си и изсмуква кръвта ѝ.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kenchrines membunuh dengan racun kuat yang membusukkan kulit, menggerogoti sendi, dan menimbulkan sakit perut hebat, atau dengan melilit dan mencekik korbannya lalu mengisap darahnya.",
        "en": "The kenchrines kills with a strong venom that rots the skin, eats away the joints and causes severe stomach pain, or by coiling round and strangling its victim and sucking its blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12282902-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q12282902-s1",
      "quote": "Напада по пладне, като жертвите му обикновено са пладнуващи овце, оставени без надзор от пастирите си, но е смъртно опасно и за човека.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyerang pada tengah hari, biasanya memangsa domba yang beristirahat siang tanpa diawasi penggembala, tetapi juga mematikan bagi manusia.",
        "en": "It attacks at noon, its victims usually being sheep resting at midday unattended by their shepherds, but it is deadly to humans as well."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kwammanga adalah tokoh pelangi dalam mitologi orang San (Bushmen), lambang kesadaran dan pikiran, yang menjaga agar banjir besar tidak terulang.",
    "en": "Kwammanga is a rainbow figure of San (Bushman) mythology, a symbol of consciousness and thought, who ensures that the flood never recurs.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q12284251-c01",
      "wd-q12284251-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi orang San (Bushmen), Kwammanga bukan manusia dan bukan hewan, melainkan bagian dari pelangi yang tinggal di dalamnya. Ia melambangkan kesadaran, pikiran, dan sisi kritis watak manusia, serta bertanggung jawab agar banjir besar tidak terjadi lagi.",
      "en": "In San (Bushman) mythology Kwammanga is neither human nor animal but part of the rainbow and lives in it. He symbolises consciousness, thought and the critical side of human character, and is responsible for ensuring that a flood never happens again.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12284251-c01",
        "wd-q12284251-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kwammanga menikah dengan Tso, seekor landak yang merupakan anak angkat dewa pencipta Cagn dan anak kandung dewa pelahap Kwai-Hemm. Mereka punya dua putra: Ni, garangan Mesir yang sering menjadi rekan Cagn, dan Kwammanga muda, pelangi yang ramah dan lembut.",
      "en": "Kwammanga is married to Tso, a porcupine who is the adopted daughter of the creator god Cagn and the natural daughter of the devourer god Kwai-Hemm. They have two sons: Ni, an Egyptian mongoose who is often Cagn's partner, and the younger Kwammanga, a kind and gentle rainbow.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12284251-c03",
        "wd-q12284251-c04",
        "wd-q12284251-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitos pembunuhan antelop, kemarau panjang melanda tanah orang San. Didorong rasa lapar, kedua Kwammanga, ayah dan anak, membunuh antelop tua walaupun tahu akan dihukum. Orang-orang memakan dagingnya tanpa menyesal, tetapi rasa bersalah kemudian menyiksa mereka sehingga mereka meminta pertolongan kepada dewa pencipta Cagn.",
      "en": "In the myth of the killing of the antelope, a long drought struck the San lands. Driven by hunger, the two Kwammangas, father and son, killed the old antelope though they knew they would be punished.
