  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "Куаманга (Kwammanga) е особен персонаж в бушменската митология. Той е този, който не е нито човек, нито животно, а е част от дъгата и живее в нея.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kwammanga adalah tokoh khusus dalam mitologi orang San (Bushmen): bukan manusia, bukan hewan, melainkan bagian dari pelangi dan tinggal di dalamnya.",
        "en": "Kwammanga is a special figure of San (Bushman) mythology: neither human nor animal, but part of the rainbow and living in it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "Той е символ на съзнанието и мисълта при бушмените, както и на критическия аспект в човешкия характер. Отговорен е и за това никога повече да не се случва потоп.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bagi orang San ia melambangkan kesadaran dan pikiran serta sisi kritis watak manusia; ia juga bertanggung jawab agar banjir besar tidak pernah terjadi lagi.",
        "en": "For the San he symbolises consciousness and thought and the critical side of human character; he is also responsible for ensuring that a flood never happens again."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "Женен е за Цо – бодливо свинче, осиновена дъщеря на бога-създател Цагн и родна дъщеря на страховития бог-поглъщач Кваи-Хему.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menikah dengan Tso, seekor landak, anak angkat dewa pencipta Cagn dan anak kandung dewa pelahap yang menakutkan, Kwai-Hemm.",
        "en": "He is married to Tso, a porcupine, adopted daughter of the creator god Cagn and natural daughter of the fearsome devourer god Kwai-Hemm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "Куаманга и Цо имат двама сина. По-големият Ни е египетска мангуста (ихневмон) и в много митове е партньор на Цагн.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kwammanga dan Tso punya dua putra; yang sulung, Ni, adalah garangan Mesir (ichneumon) dan dalam banyak mitos menjadi rekan Cagn.",
        "en": "Kwammanga and Tso have two sons; the elder, Ni, is an Egyptian mongoose (ichneumon) and in many myths a partner of Cagn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "По-малкият, който носи името на баща си Куаманга, също е дъга и е мил, безгрижен, с нежна душа и тяло.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Putra bungsu, yang bernama sama dengan ayahnya, Kwammanga, juga berupa pelangi dan bersifat ramah, riang, berjiwa dan bertubuh lembut.",
        "en": "The younger, named after his father Kwammanga, is also a rainbow and is kind, carefree and gentle in soul and body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "Една година суша застигнала земите на бушмените, много време не паднала и капчица дъжд, земята изсъхнала и се напукала.",
      "locator": "Митът за убийството на антилопата",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitos pembunuhan antelop, suatu tahun kemarau melanda tanah orang San; lama tak turun setetes hujan, dan tanah mengering serta retak.",
        "en": "In the myth of the killing of the antelope, one year drought struck the San lands; for a long time not a drop of rain fell, and the earth dried and cracked."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "Те знаели, че ако я убият ще бъдат наказани, но гладът ги принудил да го направят.",
      "locator": "Митът за убийството на антилопата",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kedua Kwammanga, ayah dan anak, tahu bahwa mereka akan dihukum bila membunuh antelop tua itu, tetapi rasa lapar memaksa mereka melakukannya.",
        "en": "The two Kwammangas, father and son, knew they would be punished if they killed the old antelope, but hunger forced them to do it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c08",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "Хората я изяли без да почувстват угризение за това, че унищожават друго живо същество, сътворено от Бога. Но вината се загнездила като бич в съзнанието им и започнала да ги мъчи.",
      "locator": "Митът за убийството на антилопата",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang-orang memakannya tanpa menyesal telah membinasakan makhluk hidup ciptaan Tuhan, tetapi rasa bersalah lalu bersarang di benak mereka dan menyiksa mereka.",
        "en": "The people ate it without remorse for destroying another living being created by God, but guilt then lodged in their minds like a scourge and began to torment them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284251-c09",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284251-s1",
      "quote": "се наложило да се обърнат за помощ към бога-творец Цагн, защото повече не можели да живеят с тази огромна вина.",
      "locator": "Митът за убийството на антилопата",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka terpaksa meminta pertolongan kepada dewa pencipta Cagn karena tak sanggup lagi hidup dengan rasa bersalah yang begitu besar.",
        "en": "They had to turn for help to the creator god Cagn, because they could no longer live with such great guilt."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kutsulan adalah serigala pincang paling berbahaya dalam kepercayaan rakyat Bulgaria, pemimpin dan penggembala kawanan serigala.",
    "en": "Kutsulan is the most dangerous lame wolf of Bulgarian folk belief, leader and shepherd of the wolf pack.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q12284431-c01",
      "wd-q12284431-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Bulgaria, Kutsulan adalah serigala yang paling berbahaya dan haus darah. Walaupun biasanya serigala penyendiri, ia dianggap pemimpin kawanan. Ia digambarkan sebagai serigala besar yang pincang, kadang sebagai lelaki tua, dan berperan sebagai penggembala para serigala.",
      "en": "In Bulgarian folk belief Kutsulan is the most dangerous and bloodthirsty wolf. Though usually a lone wolf, he is regarded as leader of the pack. He is pictured as a big lame wolf, sometimes as an old man, and acts as shepherd of the wolves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12284431-c01",
        "wd-q12284431-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut kepercayaan, Tuhan pernah memberi waktu kepada serigala untuk makan, tetapi seekor serigala pincang terlambat dan tetap lapar. Ia mendapati seorang lelaki yang sedang memetik buah dan ingin memakannya; keduanya meminta keputusan Tuhan, yang mengizinkan serigala memakan lelaki itu. Sejak itu Kutsulan menjadi yang paling berbahaya bagi manusia dan hewan. Namanya juga dipakai untuk hari terakhir Hari Raya Serigala.",
      "en": "According to belief, God once gave the wolves time to eat, but one lame wolf came too late and stayed hungry. He found a man picking fruit and wanted to eat him; they asked God to decide, and God allowed the wolf to eat the man. Since then Kutsulan has been the most dangerous to people and animals. His name is also given to the last day of the Wolf Holidays.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12284431-c03",
        "wd-q12284431-c04",

  "cultural_context": null,

  "story_mode": {

  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [
    {
      "title": {
        "id": "Serigala pincang yang terlambat",
        "en": "The lame wolf who came late"
      },
      "role": {
        "id": "Serigala yang lapar",
        "en": "The hungry wolf"
      },
      "summary": {
        "id": "Serigala pincang yang terlambat makan meminta keputusan Tuhan untuk memakan seorang lelaki; Tuhan mengizinkannya, dan sejak itu ia menjadi serigala paling berbahaya.",
        "en": "A lame wolf who was too late to eat asks God to let him eat a man; God allows it, and since then he has been the most dangerous wolf."
      },
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12284431-c03",
        "wd-q12284431-c04",
        "wd-q12284431-c05",
        "wd-q12284431-c06"
      ]

  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [
    {
      "id": "Mengapa kisah ini menjelaskan bahaya serigala lewat keputusan Tuhan?",
      "en": "Why does this tale explain the danger of wolves through God's decision?"
    }
  ],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284431-s1",
      "url": "https://bg.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9A%D1%83%D1%86%D1%83%D0%BB%D0%B0%D0%BD",
      "title": "Куцулан",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (bg)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "bg",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284431-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284431-s1",
      "quote": "в българските народни вярвания е името на най-опасния и кръвожаден вълк. Въпреки че това обикновено е вълкът-единак, той е приеман като водач на вълчата глутница.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Bulgaria, Kutsulan adalah nama serigala yang paling berbahaya dan haus darah; meski biasanya serigala penyendiri, ia dianggap pemimpin kawanan serigala.",
        "en": "In Bulgarian folk belief Kutsulan is the name of the most dangerous and bloodthirsty wolf; though usually a lone wolf, he is regarded as leader of the wolf pack."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284431-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284431-s1",
      "quote": "Представян е като голям куц вълк, но понякога и като стар човек и му се отрежда ролята на пастир на вълците.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai serigala besar yang pincang, tetapi kadang sebagai lelaki tua, dan diberi peran sebagai penggembala para serigala.",
        "en": "He is pictured as a big lame wolf, but sometimes as an old man, and is given the role of shepherd of the wolves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284431-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284431-s1",
      "quote": "Според поверието някога Господ дал време на вълците да ядат. Всички вълци успели да грабнат някакво животно за плячка, само един куц вълк не успял да стигне навреме и останал гладен.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan, dahulu Tuhan memberi waktu kepada para serigala untuk makan; semua serigala mendapat mangsa, kecuali seekor serigala pincang yang tidak tiba tepat waktu dan tetap lapar.",
        "en": "According to belief, God once gave the wolves time to eat; all the wolves seized some prey except one lame wolf, who did not arrive in time and stayed hungry."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284431-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284431-s1",
      "quote": "Спрял под едно дърво, на което имало човек, берящ плодове и поискал да го изяде. Човекът отвърнал, че това не е правилно и се допитали до Бог.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berhenti di bawah pohon tempat seorang lelaki memetik buah dan ingin memakannya; lelaki itu menjawab bahwa itu tidak benar, lalu keduanya meminta keputusan Tuhan.",
        "en": "He stopped under a tree where a man was picking fruit and wanted to eat him; the man replied that this was not right, and they asked God to decide."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284431-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284431-s1",
      "quote": "Господ след като ги изслушал отсъдил, че вълкът може да изяде човека, което вълкът и сторил.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah mendengarkan keduanya, Tuhan memutuskan bahwa serigala boleh memakan manusia itu, dan serigala pun melakukannya.",
        "en": "After hearing them, God ruled that the wolf might eat the man, which the wolf did."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284431-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284431-s1",
      "quote": "Оттогава този вълк (Куцулан) е най-опасният за хората и животните.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak itu serigala ini (Kutsulan) menjadi yang paling berbahaya bagi manusia dan hewan.",
        "en": "Since then this wolf (Kutsulan) has been the most dangerous to people and animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284431-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284431-s1",
      "quote": "Куцулан се нарича също и последният ден от Вълчите празници.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kutsulan juga nama hari terakhir dari Hari Raya Serigala (Valchi praznitsi).",
        "en": "Kutsulan is also the name of the last day of the Wolf Holidays (Valchi praznitsi)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lehusa adalah roh dalam kepercayaan rakyat Bulgaria yang mendatangi ibu baru melahirkan hingga hari ke-40 dan bisa membawa penyakit, kegilaan, atau kematian.",
    "en": "Lehusi are spirits of Bulgarian folk belief that visit new mothers until the 40th day and can bring illness, madness or death.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q12284837-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lehusa, juga leusa atau levusa, adalah sebutan dialek Bulgaria untuk perempuan yang baru melahirkan. Kata ini berasal dari kata Yunani eleousa, \"yang penuh belas kasih\", yang sering dipakai untuk Bunda Maria.",
      "en": "Lehusa, also leusa or levusa, is a Bulgarian dialect word for a woman who has just given birth. It comes from the Greek eleousa, \"merciful\", often used for the Mother of God.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12284837-c01",
        "wd-q12284837-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Bulgaria, lehusa juga roh yang mendatangi ibu baru hingga hari ke-40 setelah persalinan. Jika tidak dicegah, mereka membawa penyakit, kegilaan, dan kematian. Karena itu ibu dan bayi tidak boleh ditinggal sendirian sampai hari tersebut, dan sesudahnya dibuat roti madu untuk menenangkan para roh.",
      "en": "In Bulgarian folk belief lehusi are also spirits that visit a new mother until the 40th day after birth. If not stopped, they bring illness, madness and death. Mother and baby are therefore never left alone until that day, and afterwards a honey loaf is made to appease the spirits.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12284837-c03",
        "wd-q12284837-c04"

  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Kepercayaan ini mengatur masa 40 hari setelah persalinan: ibu dan bayi selalu ditemani dan roh-roh ditenangkan dengan roti madu.",
    "en": "The belief shapes the 40 days after childbirth: mother and baby are always accompanied and the spirits are appeased with a honey loaf.",

      "source_id": "wd-q12284837-s1",
      "quote": "Лехуса, още леуса или левуса (на гръцки: Ελεούσα, Елеуса), е българско диалектно название на родилката.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lehusa, juga leusa atau levusa (bahasa Yunani: Eleousa), adalah sebutan dialek Bulgaria untuk perempuan yang baru melahirkan.",
        "en": "Lehusa, also leusa or levusa (Greek: Eleousa), is a Bulgarian dialect term for a woman who has just given birth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284837-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284837-s1",
      "quote": "Произлиза от гръцката дума елеуса, в превод милостива, с която често се назовава Пресвета Богородица.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ini berasal dari kata Yunani eleousa yang berarti \"yang penuh belas kasih\", sebutan yang sering dipakai untuk Bunda Maria.",
        "en": "It comes from the Greek word eleousa, meaning \"merciful\", often used to name the Most Holy Mother of God."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284837-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284837-s1",
      "quote": "Според българските народни вярвания лехусите са и духове, които идват при родилките до 40-ия ден след раждането. Ако не бъдат спрени, донасят болести, лудост, смърт.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan rakyat Bulgaria, lehusa juga roh-roh yang mendatangi ibu yang baru melahirkan hingga hari ke-40 setelah persalinan; jika tidak dicegah, mereka membawa penyakit, kegilaan, dan kematian.",
        "en": "According to Bulgarian folk belief, lehusi are also spirits that visit new mothers until the 40th day after birth; if not stopped, they bring illness, madness and death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12284837-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q12284837-s1",
      "quote": "Затова при майката и бебето до този ден винаги трябва да има някого, а след изтичане на 40тия ден се омесва медена пита, за да ги умилостиви.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena itu, ibu dan bayi harus selalu ditemani seseorang sampai hari itu, dan setelah hari ke-40 berlalu dibuatlah roti madu untuk menenangkan para roh.",
        "en": "Therefore someone must always be with the mother and baby until that day, and after the 40th day a honey loaf is kneaded to appease the spirits."
      }
    }

      "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Bulgaria, Mratinyak atau mrata adalah demon jahat yang menyebabkan penyakit pada ayam dan unggas, yang juga disebut penyakit ayam; ia adalah personifikasi penyakit unggas. Ia digambarkan sebagai ayam betina hitam besar yang sangat buruk rupa, bersayap besar dan bermata sangat besar.   "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Roh pelayan rumah adalah makhluk kecil bertopi merah dalam kepercayaan sebagian daerah Balkan yang memenuhi keinginan tuannya, tetapi berbalik jahat bila diabaikan.",
    "en": "The household service spirit is a small red-capped creature of belief in parts of the Balkans that grants its master's wishes but turns hostile if neglected.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q12751029-c01",
      "wd-q12751029-c05",
      "wd-q12751029-c10",
      "wd-q12751029-c11"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Di beberapa daerah Balkan ada kepercayaan tentang roh pelayan atau roh rumah, yang juga disebut malić, macić, domaći, škrat, atau špiritus. Makhluk ini sangat kecil, sebesar ibu jari atau setinggi anak kecil, mengenakan celana čakšire dan selalu bertopi merah. Ia biasanya tinggal di cerobong, di bawah meja, atau di bawah perkakas dapur.",
      "en": "In some parts of the Balkans there was a belief in a service or house spirit, also called malić, macić, domaći, škrat or špiritus. It is very small, the size of a thumb or of a small child, wears čakšire trousers and always a red cap. It usually lives in the chimney, under the table or under kitchen vessels.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12751029-c01",
        "wd-q12751029-c02",
        "wd-q12751029-c04",
        "wd-q12751029-c05",
        "wd-q12751029-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Roh pelayan bisa dibeli di apotek, dikemas dalam botol, atau ditetaskan dari telur ayam jantan berumur sembilan tahun atau ayam betina yang berkokok. Telur itu harus dibawa di bawah ketiak kiri selama empat puluh hari, sambil duduk di belakang tungku tanpa mencukur rambut dan janggut. Roh yang menetas mampu memenuhi setiap keinginan tuannya.",
      "en": "A service spirit could be bought at an apothecary, packed in a bottle, or hatched from an egg laid by a nine-year-old cock or a crowing hen. The egg had to be carried under the left armpit for forty days while sitting behind the stove without cutting hair or shaving. The spirit that hatched could fulfil every wish of its master.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12751029-c07",
        "wd-q12751029-c08",
        "wd-q12751029-c09",
        "wd-q12751029-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bila pemiliknya lalai, roh ini berubah dari sahabat menjadi musuh berbahaya yang berusaha mencelakakan bekas pemiliknya. Ia hampir tidak bisa dimusnahkan; satu-satunya cara adalah menenggelamkannya dalam minyak mendidih. Bangsa lain mengenal makhluk serupa, seperti kobold di Jerman dan domovoi di Rusia.",
      "en": "If neglected, this spirit turned from friend into a dangerous enemy seeking to ruin its former owner. It could hardly be destroyed; the only way was to plunge it into boiling oil. Other peoples know similar beings, such as the kobold in Germany and the domovoi in Russia.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q12751029-c03",
        "wd-q12751029-c11",
        "wd-q12751029-c12"
      ]
    }

      "title": "Домаћи услужни дух",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "У неким крајевима Балкана постојало је веровање у необично створење које се називало услужни или кућни дух.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di beberapa daerah Balkan ada kepercayaan tentang makhluk aneh yang disebut roh pelayan atau roh rumah.",
        "en": "In some parts of the Balkans there was a belief in an unusual creature called the service spirit or house spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Негде се овај дух звао и малић, мацић, домаћи, шкрат, а понегде шпиритус.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di beberapa tempat roh ini juga disebut malić, macić, domaći, škrat, dan di tempat lain špiritus.",
        "en": "In some places this spirit was also called malić, macić, domaći, škrat, and elsewhere špiritus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "И други народи знају за услужног духа. У Немачкој га називају коболд, у Енглеској гуд фелоу, Шведској спирит, а у Русији домовој.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bangsa lain juga mengenal roh pelayan: di Jerman disebut kobold, di Inggris good fellow, di Swedia spirit, dan di Rusia domovoi.",
        "en": "Other peoples also know the service spirit: in Germany it is called kobold, in England good fellow, in Sweden spirit, and in Russia domovoi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Услужни дух је сасвим малог раста. Негде кажу да је величине палца, а негде да може да нарасте до висине малог детета.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh pelayan bertubuh sangat kecil; ada yang menyebutnya sebesar ibu jari, ada pula yang menyebut ia bisa tumbuh setinggi anak kecil.",
        "en": "The service spirit is very small; some say it is the size of a thumb, others that it can grow to the height of a small child."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Обучен је у чакшире, а на глави обавезно носи црвену капицу.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengenakan celana čakšire dan selalu memakai topi kecil merah di kepalanya.",
        "en": "It wears čakšire trousers and always has a little red cap on its head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Најчешће обитава у оџаку, под столом или испод каквог суда и кухињи.",
      "locator": "Место обитавања",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia paling sering tinggal di cerobong, di bawah meja, atau di bawah perkakas di dapur.",
        "en": "It most often lives in the chimney, under the table or under some vessel in the kitchen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Наиме некада су се разне магије куповале у апотекама, па је тако понегде могао да се купи и услужни дух, и то уредно спакован у флаши.",
      "locator": "Начин да се дође до услужног духа",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dahulu berbagai sihir dibeli di apotek, sehingga di beberapa tempat roh pelayan juga bisa dibeli, dikemas rapi dalam botol.",
        "en": "Formerly various magic was bought at apothecaries, so in some places a service spirit could also be bought, neatly packed in a bottle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c08",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Требало се само домоћи јајета које је снео деветогодишњи петао или кокошка која је почела да кукуриче.",
      "locator": "Начин да се дође до услужног духа",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cara lain adalah mendapatkan telur yang dierami oleh ayam jantan berumur sembilan tahun atau oleh ayam betina yang mulai berkokok.",
        "en": "Another way was to obtain an egg laid by a nine-year-old cock or by a hen that had begun to crow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c09",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Такво јаје човек је морао да носи под левим пазухом четрдесет дана, да седи иза пећи, не шиша се и не брије.",
      "locator": "Начин да се дође до услужног духа",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Telur itu harus dibawa di bawah ketiak kiri selama empat puluh hari, sambil duduk di belakang tungku, tanpa mencukur rambut dan janggut.",
        "en": "Such an egg had to be carried under the left armpit for forty days, sitting behind the stove, without cutting one's hair or shaving."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c10",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Овај кућни патуљак био је у стању да испуни сваку жељу свом господару, чини му разне услуге и створи све што би овај замислио.",
      "locator": "Начин да се дође до услужног духа",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kurcaci rumah ini mampu memenuhi setiap keinginan tuannya, memberinya berbagai layanan, dan menciptakan apa pun yang dibayangkan tuannya.",
        "en": "This house dwarf could fulfil every wish of its master, do him all kinds of services and create whatever he imagined."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c11",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Уколико би власник био нехајан према кућном духу, он би се одметнуо од њега. Тада би се од пријатеља и доброчинитеља преобразио у опасног и опаког непријатеља који је гледао да свог бившег власника на сваки начин упропасти.",
      "locator": "Начин да се дође до услужног духа",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jika pemiliknya lalai, roh rumah akan berbalik melawannya, berubah dari sahabat dan penolong menjadi musuh berbahaya dan jahat yang berusaha mencelakakan bekas pemiliknya dengan segala cara.",
        "en": "If the owner neglected the house spirit, it would turn against him, changing from friend and benefactor into a dangerous, wicked enemy seeking to ruin its former owner in every way."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q12751029-c12",
      "source_id": "wd-q12751029-s1",
      "quote": "Једини начин да се уништи био је да се потопи у врело уље, што није било нимало лако.",
      "locator": "Начин да се дође до услужног духа",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu-satunya cara memusnahkannya adalah menenggelamkannya dalam minyak mendidih, yang sama sekali tidak mudah.",
        "en": "The only way to destroy it was to plunge it into boiling oil, which was not at all easy."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Valatouny adalah gadis-gadis raksasa berkekuatan adimanusiawi dalam mitologi Belarus, yang dikaitkan dengan banyak bukit dan dikenang sebagai pejuang.",
    "en": "The valatouny are giant maidens of superhuman strength in Belarusian mythology, linked with many hills and remembered as warriors.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q17047177-c01",
      "wd-q17047177-c02",
      "wd-q17047177-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, valatouny adalah gadis-gadis raksasa berkekuatan adimanusiawi. Banyak tempat di Belarus dikaitkan dengan mereka, seperti bukit Darotsina, bukit Bahatyr, dan bukit Dziavochaya. Cekungan pada batu-batu besar sering dijelaskan sebagai jejak kaki mereka.",
      "en": "In Belarusian mythology the valatouny are giant maidens of superhuman strength. Many places in Belarus are linked with them, such as Darotsina hill, Bahatyr hill and Dziavochaya hill. Hollows in boulders were often explained as their footprints.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047177-c01",
        "wd-q17047177-c02",
        "wd-q17047177-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Darota tinggal di bukit yang kini menyandang namanya, saling melempar batang kayu dengan gadis raksasa dari bukit Piarun, dan gugur melawan pasukan musuh. Valatouna dari bukit Bahatyr setiap pagi mengambil air sejauh 10 versta dalam ember berisi 30 ember biasa; ketika orang Tatar menyerangnya, ia menewaskan hampir semuanya dengan pikulan sebelum gugur, dan air dari embernya membentuk Sungai Bahatyrka.",
      "en": "Darota lived on the hill that now bears her name, tossed logs with a giant maiden from Piarun hill and died fighting an enemy army. The valatouna of Bahatyr hill fetched water every morning from 10 versts away in tubs holding 30 buckets each; when the Tatars attacked her she struck down almost all of them with her yoke before dying, and the water from her tubs formed the Bahatyrka river.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047177-c03",
        "wd-q17047177-c04",
        "wd-q17047177-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam legenda lain, Katsiaryna dilamar dua raksasa, Stsiapan dan Marka, dan berjanji menikahi yang melempar batu paling jauh. Ketika Marka yang tak dicintainya menang, ia melompat dari batu, berubah menjadi air, dan muncul kembali sebagai mata air Sini kalodzezh 5 versta dari Prapoisk.",
      "en": "In another legend Katsiaryna was wooed by two giants, Stsiapan and Marka, and promised to marry whoever threw a boulder farther. When Marka, whom she did not love, won, she leapt from the boulder, turned into water and re-emerged as the Sini kalodzezh spring 5 versts from Prapoisk.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047177-c09",
        "wd-q17047177-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut E. Zaikouski, pemujaan kuno valatouny mirip pemujaan Dewi Ibu, tetapi kebanyakan kisah mengaitkan mereka dengan kehancuran, perang, dan kematian, bukan dengan kesuburan. Padanan mereka mungkin dewi Kelt Morrígan dan para valkyrie Skandinavia.",
      "en": "According to E. Zaikouski, the ancient cult of the valatouny resembled that of the Mother Goddess, but most tales link them with destruction, war and death rather than fertility. They may correspond to the Celtic goddess Morrígan and the Scandinavian valkyries.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047177-c07",
        "wd-q17047177-c08"
      ]
    }

    "en": "E. Zaikouski interprets the valatouny as the destructive side of the Mother Goddess, linked in the tales with war and death.",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "Валатоўны — у беларускай міфалогіі дзяўчаты-волаты са звышчалавечай сілай.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, valatouny adalah gadis-gadis raksasa (volat) berkekuatan adimanusiawi.",
        "en": "In Belarusian mythology the valatouny are giant maidens (volaty) of superhuman strength."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "З персанажамі валатовен у Беларусі звязаны многія геаграфічныя аб’екты: Дароціна гара (Дрысенскі р-н), гара Багатыр (Расонскі р-н), Дзявочая гара (Мсціслаўскі, Валожынскі р-н).",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banyak tempat di Belarus dikaitkan dengan valatouny: bukit Darotsina (distrik Drysa), bukit Bahatyr (distrik Rasony), dan bukit Dziavochaya (distrik Mstsislau dan Valozhyn).",
        "en": "Many places in Belarus are linked with the valatouny: Darotsina hill (Drysa district), Bahatyr hill (Rasony district) and Dziavochaya hill (Mstsislau and Valozhyn districts)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "Дарота — дзяўчына-волат, жыла на ўзвышшы, якое зараз завецца Дароцінай гарой. Перакідвалася бярвеннямі з дзяўчынай-волатам з Пярунавай гары. Змагаецца з варожым войскам, але гіне.",
      "locator": "Вядомыя валатоўны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Darota adalah gadis raksasa yang tinggal di bukit yang kini disebut bukit Darotsina; ia saling melempar batang kayu dengan gadis raksasa dari bukit Piarun, dan bertempur melawan pasukan musuh hingga gugur.",
        "en": "Darota is a giant maiden who lived on the height now called Darotsina hill; she tossed logs back and forth with a giant maiden from Piarun hill, and fought an enemy army but died."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "Кожную раніцу хадзіла па ваду за 10 вёрст і насіла яе цэбрамі, у кожным з якіх памясцілася б па 30 вёдзер.",
      "locator": "Вядомыя валатоўны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Valatouna dari bukit Bahatyr setiap pagi mengambil air sejauh 10 versta dan membawanya dalam ember-ember yang masing-masing memuat 30 ember biasa.",
        "en": "The valatouna of Bahatyr hill went every morning 10 versts for water and carried it in tubs each holding 30 buckets."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "Між тым татары ўбачылі з вяршыні Багатыр-гары Святую Полацкую Сафію і накінуліся на валатоўну. Яна перабіла каромыслам амаль усіх, але і сама загінула. З цэбраў пацякла вада, ад якой утварылася рэчка Багатырка.",
      "locator": "Вядомыя валатоўны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Tatar melihat Katedral Santa Sofia Polotsk dari puncak bukit Bahatyr lalu menyerang valatouna; ia menewaskan hampir semuanya dengan pikulannya tetapi ikut gugur, dan air dari embernya mengalir membentuk Sungai Bahatyrka.",
        "en": "The Tatars saw Saint Sophia of Polotsk from the top of Bahatyr hill and fell upon the valatouna; she struck down almost all of them with her yoke but died herself, and the water from her tubs flowed out to form the Bahatyrka river."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "Паглыбленні на валунах нярэдка тлумачылі як водціскі ад ног старажытных дзяўчат-вяліканак.",
      "locator": "Сляды валатовен",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cekungan pada batu-batu besar sering dijelaskan sebagai jejak kaki gadis-gadis raksasa zaman dahulu.",
        "en": "Hollows in boulders were often explained as footprints of the ancient giant maidens."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "Як лічыць Э. Зайкоўскі, старажытны культ валатовен быў падобны да культу Багіні-Маці. Большасць сюжэтаў, аднак, звязвае дзяўчат-вяліканак не з творчай сілай (урадлівасць, плоднасць), а з разбурэннем, вайной і смерцю.",
      "locator": "Інтэпрэтацыя",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut E. Zaikouski, pemujaan kuno valatouny mirip pemujaan Dewi Ibu; namun sebagian besar kisah mengaitkan gadis raksasa ini bukan dengan daya cipta (kesuburan), melainkan dengan kehancuran, perang, dan kematian.",
        "en": "According to E. Zaikouski, the ancient cult of the valatouny resembled that of the Mother Goddess; yet most tales link the giant maidens not with creative power (fertility) but with destruction, war and death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c08",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "Ім можа адпавядаць кельцкая багіня Морыган і скандынаўскія ваяўнічыя дзевы-валькірыі.",
      "locator": "Інтэпрэтацыя",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Padanan mereka mungkin dewi Kelt Morrígan dan para valkyrie, gadis pejuang Skandinavia.",
        "en": "They may correspond to the Celtic goddess Morrígan and the Scandinavian warrior maidens, the valkyries."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c09",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "У другім паданні за Кацярыну сватаюцца 2 волаты — Сцяпан і Марка. Дзяўчына-волат абяцае выйсці за таго, хто кіне валун далей.",
      "locator": "Вядомыя валатоўны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda lain, gadis raksasa Katsiaryna dilamar oleh 2 raksasa, Stsiapan dan Marka, dan berjanji menikahi yang melempar batu besar paling jauh.",
        "en": "In another legend the giant maiden Katsiaryna is wooed by 2 giants, Stsiapan and Marka, and promises to marry whoever throws a boulder farther."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047177-c10",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047177-s1",
      "quote": "Перамагае нялюбы ёй Марка, і Кацярына, каб пазбегнуць шлюбу з ім, кідаецца з валуна, разлілася вадою, якая знікла ў зямлі і выйшла на свет крыніцай за 5 вёрст ад Прапойска. Тую крыніцу празвалі «Сіні калодзеж».",
      "locator": "Вядомыя валатоўны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Marka yang tidak dicintainya menang; untuk menghindari pernikahan, Katsiaryna melompat dari batu, berubah menjadi air yang lenyap ke tanah dan muncul sebagai mata air 5 versta dari Prapoisk, yang dinamai \"Sini kalodzezh\" (Sumur Biru).",
        "en": "Marka, whom she did not love, won; to escape marrying him Katsiaryna threw herself from the boulder, turned into water that vanished into the earth and came out as a spring 5 versts from Prapoisk, called \"Sini kalodzezh\" (the Blue Well)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vetser adalah personifikasi angin dalam mitologi Belarus: lelaki gemuk buruk rupa bermulut seperti paruh angsa yang meniupkan angin.",
    "en": "Vetser is the personified wind of Belarusian mythology: an ugly fat man with a goose-beak mouth who blows the wind.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q17047194-c01",
      "wd-q17047194-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Vetser adalah angin yang dipersonifikasikan. Ia digambarkan sebagai lelaki gemuk buruk rupa dengan mulut seperti paruh angsa. Untuk menimbulkan angin kencang ia membuka paruhnya lebar-lebar, sedangkan untuk angin dingin ia merapatkannya dan hanya menyisakan lubang kecil. Orang Belarus percaya bahwa tukang giling, yang dianggap tukang sihir, harus mampu \"memasang kekang pada angin\" dan memanggilnya saat dibutuhkan.",
      "en": "In Belarusian mythology Vetser is the personified wind. He is described as an ugly fat man with a mouth like a goose's beak. To raise a strong wind he opens his beak wide, while for a cold wind he presses it tight, leaving only a small opening. Belarusians believed that millers, who were considered sorcerers, had to be able to \"harness the wind\" and summon it when needed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047194-c01",
        "wd-q17047194-c02",
        "wd-q17047194-c03"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "wd-q17047194-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047194-s1",
      "quote": "Вецер - у беларускай міфалогіі персаніфікаваная прыродная з'ява. Апісваўся як пачварны тоўсты чалавек з ротам, падобным да гусінай дзюбы.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Vetser (Angin) adalah gejala alam yang dipersonifikasikan, digambarkan sebagai lelaki gemuk buruk rupa dengan mulut seperti paruh angsa.",
        "en": "In Belarusian mythology Vetser (the Wind) is a personified natural phenomenon, described as an ugly fat man with a mouth like a goose's beak."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047194-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047194-s1",
      "quote": "Каб выклікаць моцны вецер, ён шырока раскрывае сваю дзюбу, а каб халодны - шчыльна сцісквае і пакідае на канцы невялічную адтуліну.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk menimbulkan angin kencang ia membuka lebar paruhnya, dan untuk angin dingin ia merapatkannya dan menyisakan lubang kecil di ujungnya.",
        "en": "To raise a strong wind he opens his beak wide, and for a cold wind he presses it tight, leaving a small opening at the tip."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047194-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047194-s1",
      "quote": "Беларусы верылі, што млынары (якія наогул лічыліся ў народзе чараўнікамі) павінны ўмець \"запрагаць вецер\", выклікаць яго ў патрэбны час.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Belarus percaya bahwa tukang giling (yang umumnya dianggap tukang sihir oleh rakyat) harus mampu \"memasang kekang pada angin\", memanggilnya pada saat dibutuhkan.",
        "en": "Belarusians believed that millers (generally considered sorcerers by the people) had to be able to \"harness the wind\", summoning it when needed."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Zhyzhal adalah dewa api bawah tanah dalam mitologi Belarus yang diajukan oleh folkloris Pavel Shpilevsky; apinya menghangatkan bumi atau menimbulkan kebakaran.",
    "en": "Zhyzhal is an underground fire god of Belarusian mythology proposed by the folklorist Pavel Shpilevsky; his fire warms the earth or causes wildfires.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q17047370-c01",
      "wd-q17047370-c02",
      "wd-q17047370-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Zhyzhal, juga Zhyzh, Zhyzha, atau Zhyzhak, adalah dewa api Belarus kuno yang diajukan oleh folkloris Pavel Shpilevsky. Shpilevsky menurunkan dewa ini dari peribahasa dan dari kata kanak-kanak untuk api, \"zhyzha\". Roh ini konon dipercaya terutama di Kegubernuran Minsk.",
      "en": "Zhyzhal, also Zhyzh, Zhyzha or Zhyzhak, is an ancient Belarusian fire god proposed by the folklorist Pavel Shpilevsky. Shpilevsky derived the deity from proverbs and from the children's word for fire, \"zhyzha\". The spirit was supposedly believed in mainly in Minsk Governorate.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047370-c01",
        "wd-q17047370-c02",
        "wd-q17047370-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut gambaran itu, Zhyzhal tinggal di bawah tanah dan mengeluarkan api. Jika perlahan, bumi menjadi hangat; jika cepat, timbul kebakaran, padang rumput dan hutan musnah, dan ladang menjadi tandus. Ungkapan \"Zhyzh sudah terbiasa datang\" berarti kekeringan atau kebakaran mulai sering terjadi. Ia juga disebut sebagai salah satu musuh Perun.",
      "en": "In this conception Zhyzhal lives underground and releases fire. When slowly, the earth warms; when quickly, fires break out, meadows and forests perish and fields become barren. The saying \"Zhyzh has taken to coming\" meant that drought or fire was becoming frequent. He is also named among the enemies of Perun.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047370-c02",
        "wd-q17047370-c03",
        "wd-q17047370-c04",
        "wd-q17047370-c06"
      ]
    }

      "source_id": "wd-q17047370-s1",
      "quote": "(Жыж, Жыжа, Жыжак) — прапанаваны фалькларыстам Паўлам Шпілеўскім старажытнабеларускі бог агню.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",

        "en": "Zhyzhal (also Zhyzh, Zhyzha, Zhyzhak) is an ancient Belarusian fire god proposed by the folklorist Pavel Shpilevsky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047370-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047370-s1",
      "quote": "У духа Жыжаля нібыта верылі ў асноўным на тэрыторыі Мінскай губерні. Паводле ўяўленняў беларусаў Жыжаль жыве пад зямлёй і там выпускае з сябе агонь.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh Zhyzhal konon dipercaya terutama di wilayah Kegubernuran Minsk; menurut gambaran orang Belarus, Zhyzhal tinggal di bawah tanah dan di sana mengeluarkan api dari dirinya.",
        "en": "The spirit Zhyzhal was supposedly believed in mainly in Minsk Governorate; in Belarusian conception Zhyzhal lives underground and releases fire from himself there."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047370-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047370-s1",
      "quote": "Калі ён робіць гэта павольна, то зямля саграваецца, а калі хутка, то ўзнікаюць на зямной паверхні пажары, гінуць сенажаці і лясы, высушваюцца палі і агароды, ад чаго яны робяцца бясплоднымі.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jika ia melakukannya perlahan, bumi menjadi hangat; jika cepat, timbul kebakaran di permukaan bumi, padang rumput dan hutan musnah, ladang dan kebun mengering hingga menjadi tandus.",
        "en": "When he does this slowly the earth warms; when quickly, fires break out on the surface, meadows and forests perish, and fields and gardens dry up and become barren."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047370-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047370-s1",
      "quote": "Існавалі выслоўі: «Жыж унадзіўся», г. зн. «сталі часта ўзнікаць засуха ці пажар»",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada ungkapan \"Zhyzh sudah terbiasa datang\", yang berarti \"kekeringan atau kebakaran mulai sering terjadi\".",
        "en": "There was a saying \"Zhyzh has taken to coming\", meaning \"drought or fire has begun to occur often\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047370-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047370-s1",
      "quote": "П. Шпілеўскі вывеў боства з прымавак і дзіцячай назвы агню «жыжа».",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "P. Shpilevsky menurunkan dewa ini dari peribahasa dan dari kata kanak-kanak untuk api, \"zhyzha\".",
        "en": "P. Shpilevsky derived the deity from proverbs and from the children's word for fire, \"zhyzha\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047370-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047370-s1",
      "quote": "Узгадваецца сярод ворагаў Пяруна.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut di antara musuh-musuh Perun.",
        "en": "He is mentioned among the enemies of Perun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047370-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047370-s1",
      "quote": "У гонар Жыжаля названы штогадовы беларускі мастацкі фестываль керамістаў і ганчароў «Арт-Жыжаль», які летам 2015 года праводзіўся ўжо ў 13 раз.",
      "locator": "У мастацтве",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Festival seni tahunan Belarus untuk perajin keramik dan tembikar, \"Art-Zhyzhal\", dinamai untuk menghormati Zhyzhal; pada musim panas 2015 festival itu sudah diadakan untuk ke-13 kalinya.",
        "en": "The annual Belarusian art festival of ceramists and potters, \"Art-Zhyzhal\", is named in honour of Zhyzhal; in summer 2015 it was held for the 13th time."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kingi adalah ratu jelita dalam legenda Belarus yang istananya amblas ke dalam bumi dan yang setiap malam keluar menyalakan api unggun.",
    "en": "Kingi is a beautiful queen of Belarusian legend whose palace sank into the earth and who comes out every night to light a bonfire.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q17047475-c01",
      "wd-q17047475-c02",
      "wd-q17047475-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Kingi adalah ratu jelita yang istananya berdiri di tepi Sungai Narew dekat muaranya ke Sungai Bug. Suatu hari, ketika ia berjalan di tamannya, bumi berguncang, istananya amblas, dan di tempatnya tumbuh bukit kecil.",
      "en": "In Belarusian mythology Kingi is a beautiful queen whose palace stood on the Narew river near its mouth on the Bug. One day, as she walked in her garden, the earth shook, the palace sank and a small hill rose in its place.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047475-c01",
        "wd-q17047475-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sejak itu ia keluar dari tanah setiap malam dan menyalakan api unggun, menunggu seseorang memetikkan bunga untuknya, tetapi orang-orang menghindari tempat itu. Ketika seorang perampok tua menyerangnya dengan pisau setelah ditawari emas, Kingi lenyap dan perampok itu berubah menjadi batu besar. Legenda ini menggabungkan alur tentang permukiman yang amblas dan manusia yang menjadi batu.",
      "en": "Since then she came out of the earth every night and lit a bonfire, waiting for someone to pick flowers for her, but people avoided the place. When an old robber, offered gold, attacked her with a knife, Kingi vanished and the robber turned into a boulder. The legend combines plots about sunken settlements and people turned to stone.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047475-c03",
        "wd-q17047475-c04",
        "wd-q17047475-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047475-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047475-s1",
      "quote": "Кінгі — у беларускай міфалогіі прыгажуня-каралева, палац якой стаяў ля ракі Нараў, недалёка ад месца ўпадзення ў Буг.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Kingi adalah ratu jelita yang istananya berdiri di tepi Sungai Narew, tidak jauh dari muaranya ke Sungai Bug.",
        "en": "In Belarusian mythology Kingi is a beautiful queen whose palace stood by the Narew river, not far from where it flows into the Bug."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047475-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047475-s1",
      "quote": "Калі прыгажуня гуляла па сваім садзе і слухала спевы птушак, зямля раптам затрэслася, і палац праваліўся ўніз, а на яго месцы вырасла невялічкая гара.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika sang ratu berjalan-jalan di tamannya sambil mendengarkan kicau burung, bumi tiba-tiba berguncang, istananya amblas, dan di tempatnya tumbuh sebuah bukit kecil.",
        "en": "As the beauty walked in her garden listening to birdsong, the earth suddenly shook, the palace sank down and a small hill rose in its place."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047475-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047475-s1",
      "quote": "З таго часу Кінгі кожную ноч выходзіла з зямлі і раскладала вогнішча, чакаючы, пакуль хтосьці з людзей нарве ёй кветак. Але людзі, пабачыўшы вогнішча здалёку, абыходзілі падазронае месца бокам.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak itu Kingi setiap malam keluar dari tanah dan menyalakan api unggun, menunggu seseorang memetikkan bunga untuknya; tetapi orang yang melihat api itu dari jauh selalu menghindari tempat yang mencurigakan itu.",
        "en": "Since then Kingi came out of the earth every night and lit a bonfire, waiting for someone to pick flowers for her; but people who saw the fire from afar avoided the suspicious place."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047475-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047475-s1",
      "quote": "Аднойчы агонь заўважыў стары разбойнік. Кінгі прапанавала яму нарваць кветак у абмен на золата. Разбойнік кінуўся на яе з нажом, але дзяўчына знікла, а злодзей ператварыўся ў валун.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suatu kali seorang perampok tua melihat api itu; Kingi menawarkan emas sebagai imbalan bunga, tetapi perampok itu menyerangnya dengan pisau, lalu sang gadis lenyap dan si pencuri berubah menjadi batu besar.",
        "en": "Once an old robber noticed the fire; Kingi offered him gold in exchange for flowers, but the robber attacked her with a knife, whereupon the maiden vanished and the thief turned into a boulder."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047475-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047475-s1",
      "quote": "Легенда спалучае адразу некалькі папулярных сюжэтаў: пра паселішчы, якія праваліліся пад зямлю; пра чалавека, які стаў камнем і г.д.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini menggabungkan beberapa alur populer sekaligus: permukiman yang amblas ke dalam tanah, manusia yang menjadi batu, dan sebagainya.",
        "en": "The legend combines several popular plots at once: settlements that sank into the earth, a man who turned to stone, and so on."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Yashchar is a hypothetical pagan serpent god, lord of the sea and underground waters, whose traces are thought to survive in a Belarusian peasant game.",

    {
      "id": "Yashchar atau Yashchur adalah dewa pagan hipotetis yang dipuja leluhur orang Belarus dan penduduk Slavia di Rus utara. Menurut A. L. Barkova, ia adalah dewa ular, penguasa laut dan air bawah tanah, dengan tempat pemujaan di dekat rawa, danau, dan sungai. Barkova juga berpendapat ia semula dewa utama orang Novgorod sampai Vladimir memaksakan pemujaan Perun.",
      "en": "Yashchar or Yashchur is a hypothetical pagan deity worshipped by the ancestors of the Belarusians and by the Slavic population of northern Rus. According to A. L. Barkova he was a serpent god, lord of the sea and underground waters, with sanctuaries near marshes, lakes and rivers. Barkova also holds that he was originally the chief god of the Novgorodians until Vladimir imposed the cult of Perun.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17094439-c01",
        "wd-q17094439-c02",

      ]

      "id": "Ayam hitam dan gadis muda konon dikurbankan kepadanya, yang tercermin dalam takhayul tentang ular yang mengambil gadis sebagai istri. Rybakov, permainan ini mungkin sisa gagasan tentang dewa ular yang memilih gadis kurban.",
      "en": "Black hens and young girls were reportedly sacrificed to him, as reflected in beliefs about a serpent taking a girl as his wife. Rybakov's view the game may be a remnant of the idea of a serpent god choosing a sacrificial girl.",

      "en": "Since the Indo-European thunder god was often a serpent-slayer, researchers consider the hypothesis of enmity between Perun and Yashchar well founded.",

  "claims": [

      "id": "wd-q17094439-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q17094439-s1",
      "quote": "Яшчар або Яшчур — гіпатэтычнае язычніцкае боства, якому пакланяліся продкі сучасных беларусаў, а таксама славянскае насельніцтва паўночнай Русі.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yashchar atau Yashchur adalah dewa pagan hipotetis yang dipuja leluhur orang Belarus modern dan penduduk Slavia di Rus utara.",
        "en": "Yashchar or Yashchur is a hypothetical pagan deity worshipped by the ancestors of modern Belarusians and by the Slavic population of northern Rus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17094439-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17094439-s1",
      "quote": "Паводле А. Л. Барковай, Яшчар — гэта бог-змей, уладар мора і падземных вод. Яго капішчы размяшчаліся ля балот, азёр і рэк.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut A. L. Barkova, Yashchar adalah dewa ular, penguasa laut dan air bawah tanah, dan tempat pemujaannya berada di dekat rawa, danau, dan sungai.",
        "en": "According to A. L. Barkova, Yashchar is a serpent god, lord of the sea and underground waters, whose sanctuaries lay near marshes, lakes and rivers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17094439-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17094439-s1",
      "quote": "У якасці ахвяр Яшчару прыносілі чорных кур і маладых дзяўчын, што адлюстравалася ў шматлікіх павер’ях, дзе змей (часцей за ўсё вуж) забірае сабе дзяўчыну і робіць сваёй жонкай.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai kurban bagi Yashchar dipersembahkan ayam hitam dan gadis muda, yang tercermin dalam banyak takhayul ketika ular (biasanya ular air) mengambil seorang gadis dan menjadikannya istri.",
        "en": "Black hens and young girls were offered to Yashchar as sacrifices, which is reflected in many beliefs where a serpent (most often a grass snake) takes a girl and makes her his wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17094439-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q17094439-s1",
      "quote": "вядома гульня ў Яшчара, дзе юнак ці хлопчык граюць ролю аднайменнага персанажа, выбіраюць сабе «нявесту».",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di kalangan petani Belarus dikenal permainan Yashchar, di mana seorang pemuda atau anak laki-laki memerankan tokoh itu dan memilih \"pengantin\" untuk dirinya.",
        "en": "Among Belarusian peasants a game of Yashchar was known, in which a youth or boy plays the character of that name and chooses a \"bride\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17094439-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q17094439-s1",
      "quote": "На думку Б. А. Рыбакова гульня можа быць рэшткай уяўлення пра змеепадобнае боства, якое выбірае сабе «жонку» — дзяўчыну дзеля ахвярапрынашэння.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut B. A. Rybakov, permainan ini mungkin sisa gagasan tentang dewa berwujud ular yang memilih \"istri\", yaitu gadis untuk dikurbankan.",
        "en": "In B. A. Rybakov's view the game may be a remnant of the idea of a serpent-like deity choosing a \"wife\", a girl to be sacrificed."

    {
      "id": "wd-q17094439-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q17094439-s1",
      "quote": "Улічваючы тое, што грамавержац у індаеўрапейскіх народаў часта з’яўляўся змееборцам, мае рацыю гіпотэза аб ворагаванні старажытных багоў Пяруна і Яшчара.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena dewa guntur bangsa-bangsa Indo-Eropa sering menjadi pembunuh ular, hipotesis tentang permusuhan antara dewa kuno Perun dan Yashchar dianggap beralasan.",
        "en": "Since the thunder god of Indo-European peoples was often a serpent-slayer, the hypothesis of enmity between the ancient gods Perun and Yashchar is considered sound."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17094439-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q17094439-s1",
      "quote": "Паводле А. Л. Барковай, Яшчар спачатку быў галоўным бозствам наўгародцаў, пакуль Уладзімір (будучы хрысціцель) не насадзіў ім культ Пяруна.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut A. L. Barkova, Yashchar awalnya dewa utama orang Novgorod sampai Vladimir (kelak pembaptis Rus) memaksakan pemujaan Perun kepada mereka.",
        "en": "According to A. L. Barkova, Yashchar was originally the chief deity of the Novgorodians until Vladimir (the future baptiser) imposed the cult of Perun on them."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Svitsiazianki adalah makhluk sejenis rusalka di Danau Svitsiaz, distrik Karelichy: perempuan yang berubah wujud karena peristiwa tragis, diabadikan dalam balada Adam Mickiewicz.",
    "en": "The svitsiazianki are rusalka-like beings of Lake Svitsiaz in Karelichy district: women transformed through tragic events, immortalised in Adam Mickiewicz's ballads.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q21720598-c01",
      "wd-q21720598-c02",
      "wd-q21720598-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Svitsiazianki adalah makhluk mitos Danau Svitsiaz di distrik Karelichy, sejenis rusalka. Legenda tentang perempuan atau gadis yang berubah menjadi svitsiazianki karena peristiwa tragis tersebar luas di sekitar danau hingga 20-30 km jauhnya. Mereka dibayangkan sebagai gadis berwajah putih dengan rambut panjang terurai.",
      "en": "The svitsiazianki are mythical beings of Lake Svitsiaz in Karelichy district, a kind of rusalka. Legends of women or girls turned into svitsiazianki through tragic events were widespread up to 20-30 km around the lake. They were imagined as white-faced girls with long, loose hair.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q21720598-c01",
        "wd-q21720598-c02",
        "wd-q21720598-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda ini menarik perhatian penyair romantik Adam Mickiewicz, yang menulis tiga balada tentangnya dalam kumpulan puisi pertamanya (1822). Dalam balada \"Svitsiaz\", perempuan kota Svitsiaz menjadi svitsiazianki setelah para dewa menenggelamkan kota itu atas permintaan mereka agar terhindar dari aib pasukan musuh; bunga-bunga yang tumbuh di danau ternyata beracun dan menewaskan para penakluk yang memetiknya.",
      "en": "The legends caught the attention of the Romantic poet Adam Mickiewicz, who wrote three ballads about them in his first poetry collection (1822). In the ballad \"Svitsiaz\", the women of the town became svitsiazianki after the gods drowned it at their request to save them from dishonour by an enemy army; the flowers that grew on the lake proved poisonous and killed the invaders who picked them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q21720598-c03",
        "wd-q21720598-c04",
        "wd-q21720598-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam balada \"Svitsiazianka\", seorang svitsiazianka yang menyamar sebagai gadis biasa menguji kesetiaan pemuda pemburu yang mencintainya. Pemuda itu mengkhianati sumpahnya dan jiwanya dihukum berkelana selamanya di sekitar danau.",
      "en": "In the ballad \"Svitsiazianka\", a svitsiazianka posing as an ordinary girl tests the faithfulness of a young hunter who loves her. He breaks his oath, and his soul is punished with eternal wandering around the lake.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q21720598-c07",
        "wd-q21720598-c08"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q21720598-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q21720598-s1",
      "quote": "міфічныя істоты возера Свіцязь у Карэліцкім раёне, адмена русалак.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Svitsiazianki adalah makhluk mitos Danau Svitsiaz di distrik Karelichy, sejenis rusalka.",
        "en": "The svitsiazianki are mythical beings of Lake Svitsiaz in Karelichy district, a kind of rusalka."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q21720598-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q21720598-s1",
      "quote": "Паданні пра свіцязянак, у якіх ператварыліся зямныя жанчыны або дзяўчаты праз пэўныя трагічныя падзеі, былі шырока распаўсюджаны на Свіцязі і ў ваколіцах за 20-30 км ад возера",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda tentang svitsiazianki, yaitu perempuan atau gadis duniawi yang berubah wujud karena peristiwa tragis, tersebar luas di Svitsiaz dan sekitarnya hingga 20-30 km dari danau.",
        "en": "Legends of the svitsiazianki, earthly women or girls transformed through tragic events, were widespread at Svitsiaz and up to 20-30 km around the lake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q21720598-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q21720598-s1",
      "quote": "прыцягнулі ўвагу маладых паэтаў-рамантыкаў з ліку філаматаў, асабліва Адама Міцкевіча, які прысвяціў Свіцязі і свіцязянкам тры балады ў сваім першым паэтычным зборнік (1822), які зрабіў яго славутым.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini menarik perhatian para penyair romantik muda dari kalangan Filomat, terutama Adam Mickiewicz, yang mempersembahkan tiga balada tentang Svitsiaz dan svitsiazianki dalam kumpulan puisi pertamanya (1822) yang membuatnya terkenal.",
        "en": "The legends drew the attention of young Romantic poets among the Philomaths, especially Adam Mickiewicz, who devoted three ballads to Svitsiaz and the svitsiazianki in his first poetry collection (1822), which made him famous."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q21720598-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q21720598-s1",
      "quote": "у свіцязянак ператварыліся жанчыны горада Свіцязі, затопленага багамі на іх просьбу, каб унікнуць ганьбавання варожым рускім войскам",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut balada pertama, perempuan-perempuan kota Svitsiaz berubah menjadi svitsiazianki ketika kota itu ditenggelamkan para dewa atas permintaan mereka agar terhindar dari aib oleh pasukan Rus yang bermusuhan.",
        "en": "In the first ballad the women of the town of Svitsiaz became svitsiazianki when the gods, at their request, drowned the town so they would escape dishonour by the hostile Rus army."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q21720598-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q21720598-s1",
      "quote": "Уяўлялі іх як белатварых дзяўчат з даўгімі распушчанымі валасамі.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dibayangkan sebagai gadis-gadis berwajah putih dengan rambut panjang terurai.",
        "en": "They were imagined as white-faced girls with long, loose hair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q21720598-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q21720598-s1",
      "quote": "Калі трыумфуючыя захопнікі пачалі зрываць кветкі («цары», паводле балады Міцкевіча «Свіцязь»), яны аказаліся атрутнымі, і ўсе яны загінулі.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika para penakluk yang berjaya mulai memetik bunga-bunga di danau (\"tsary\" dalam balada Mickiewicz \"Svitsiaz\"), bunga itu ternyata beracun dan mereka semua tewas.",
        "en": "When the triumphant invaders began to pick the flowers on the lake (\"tsary\" in Mickiewicz's ballad \"Svitsiaz\"), they proved poisonous and all of them died."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q21720598-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q21720598-s1",
      "quote": "расказвае пра выпрабаванне, якому свіцязянка, якая спачатку выдае сябе за звычайную дзяўчыну, паддае закаханага ў яе юнака-стральца.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Balada kedua, \"Svitsiazianka\", menceritakan ujian yang diberikan seorang svitsiazianka, yang mula-mula menyamar sebagai gadis biasa, kepada seorang pemuda pemburu yang jatuh cinta kepadanya.",
        "en": "The second ballad, \"Svitsiazianka\", tells of the test a svitsiazianka, first posing as an ordinary girl, sets a young hunter in love with her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q21720598-c08",
      "source_id": "wd-q21720598-s1",
      "quote": "Аднак здрада была здзейснена, і душа маладога чалавек, які гіне, пакараная вечным бадзяннем у наваколлі.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengkhianatan pemuda itu terjadi, dan jiwanya yang binasa dihukum berkelana selamanya di sekitar danau.",
        "en": "The betrayal was committed, and the soul of the young man, who perishes, is punished with eternal wandering in the surroundings."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vud-Ava (\"Ibu Air\") adalah dewi dalam mitologi Mari, penguasa semua perairan daratan yang memberi tangkapan ikan melimpah dan menjaga kebersihan air.",
    "en": "Vud-Ava (\"Mother of Water\") is a goddess of Mari mythology, mistress of all inland waters who grants rich catches and keeps the waters clean.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q4128235-c01",
      "wd-q4128235-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vud-Ava, \"Ibu Air\", adalah dewi dalam mitologi Mari yang menguasai semua perairan daratan. Menurut sebagian keterangan ia tinggal di air mana pun, menurut yang lain hanya di perairan dalam. Pada musim semi ia mengaduk lumpur dari dasar, sedangkan pada musim gugur ia mencampur air dengan madu sehingga terasa enak. Sebagai leluhur segala sesuatu, ia dianggap dewi tertua dan personifikasi kebijaksanaan.",
      "en": "Vud-Ava, \"Mother of Water\", is a goddess of Mari mythology who rules all inland waters. Some accounts say she lives in any water, others only in deep waters. In spring she stirred up silt from the bottom, while in autumn she mixed the water with honey to make it tasty. As ancestress of all things she was considered the oldest goddess and the embodiment of wisdom.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q4128235-c01",
        "wd-q4128235-c02",
        "wd-q4128235-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Vud-Ava memberikan tangkapan ikan yang melimpah dan menjaga kebersihan perairan, melarang orang membuang air kotor, mencuci pakaian, atau menumpuk sampah di dekat sungai, danau, dan mata air. Pohon di dekat air tidak pernah ditebang karena ia mencintainya, dan pelanggar bisa kehilangan keberuntungan memancing selamanya. Ia juga bisa \"menyembunyikan\" danau di taiga sehingga danau baru yang serupa muncul di hutan.",
      "en": "Vud-Ava granted rich catches and kept the waters clean, forbidding people to pour out slops, wash laundry or pile rubbish by rivers, lakes and springs. Trees near water were never felled because she loved them, and offenders might lose their fishing luck forever. She could also \"hide\" a lake in the taiga, so that an identical new one appeared in the forest.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q4128235-c03",
        "wd-q4128235-c04",
        "wd-q4128235-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dibayangkan sebagai perempuan telanjang berambut panjang yang menyisir rambutnya dengan sisir emas, dan kadang mengambil wujud ikan tombak.",
      "en": "She was imagined as a naked woman with long hair who combs it with a golden comb, and she sometimes took the form of a pike.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q4128235-c06",
        "wd-q4128235-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Kepercayaan kepada Vud-Ava disertai larangan mencemari sungai, danau, dan mata air serta menebang pohon di dekat air.",
    "en": "Belief in Vud-Ava came with prohibitions against polluting rivers, lakes and springs and against felling trees near water.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q4128235-c03",

  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "url": "https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%92%D1%83%D0%B4-%D0%90%D0%B2%D0%B0",
      "title": "Вуд-Ава",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ru)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ru",
      "type": "wikipedia",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "quote": "(луговомар. Вӱд Ава, Мать воды) — богиня в марийской мифологии, хозяйка всех континентальных водных пространств.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vud-Ava (bahasa Mari Padang: Vüd Ava, \"Ibu Air\") adalah dewi dalam mitologi Mari, penguasa semua perairan daratan.",
        "en": "Vud-Ava (Meadow Mari Vüd Ava, \"Mother of Water\") is a goddess of Mari mythology, mistress of all inland waters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "quote": "Обитает, по одним данным, в любой воде, по другим, только в глубоких водах. Весной подымала муть со дна, осенью же, напротив, смешивала воду с мёдом, делая её особенно вкусной.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sebagian keterangan ia tinggal di air mana pun, menurut yang lain hanya di perairan dalam; pada musim semi ia mengaduk lumpur dari dasar, dan pada musim gugur mencampur air dengan madu sehingga terasa sangat enak.",
        "en": "By some accounts she lives in any water, by others only in deep waters; in spring she stirred up silt from the bottom, while in autumn she mixed the water with honey, making it especially tasty."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "quote": "обеспечивала богатый улов, следила за чистотой водоёмов: запрещала сливать помои, стирать бельё, устраивать свалки у рек, озёр, родников.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memberikan tangkapan ikan yang melimpah dan menjaga kebersihan perairan: ia melarang membuang air kotor, mencuci pakaian, dan membuang sampah di dekat sungai, danau, dan mata air.",
        "en": "She ensured rich catches and kept the waters clean, forbidding the dumping of slops, washing laundry or making rubbish heaps by rivers, lakes and springs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "quote": "Никогда не уничтожались деревья вблизи воды — они считались особо любимыми Вӱд-Авой. За это могло последовать наказание в виде лишения навсегда удачи в рыбалке.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pohon di dekat air tidak pernah ditebang karena dianggap sangat dicintai Vud-Ava; pelanggarnya bisa dihukum dengan kehilangan keberuntungan memancing untuk selamanya.",
        "en": "Trees near water were never destroyed, as they were considered especially dear to Vud-Ava; the punishment could be losing one's luck in fishing forever."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "quote": "Она могла «спрятать» воду в глубине тайги: озеро исчезало, а в тот же год охотники находили в лесу новое такое же.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bisa \"menyembunyikan\" air di kedalaman taiga: sebuah danau lenyap, dan pada tahun yang sama para pemburu menemukan danau baru yang serupa di hutan.",
        "en": "She could \"hide\" water deep in the taiga: a lake would vanish, and that same year hunters would find an identical new one in the forest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "quote": "Богиню представляли в виде обнажённой женщины с длинными волосами, которые она любит расчёсывать золотым гребнем.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewi ini dibayangkan sebagai perempuan telanjang berambut panjang yang suka menyisir rambutnya dengan sisir emas.",
        "en": "The goddess was imagined as a naked woman with long hair, which she likes to comb with a golden comb."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "quote": "Иногда богиня могла принимать образ щуки.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang dewi ini bisa mengambil wujud ikan tombak (pike).",
        "en": "Sometimes the goddess could take the form of a pike."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4128235-c08",
      "source_id": "wd-q4128235-s1",
      "quote": "Как родоначальница всего, Вӱд-Ава считалась старейшей богиней, олицетворением мудрости.",
      "locator": "Мифы. Образ в народной поэзии",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai leluhur segala sesuatu, Vud-Ava dianggap dewi tertua dan personifikasi kebijaksanaan.",
        "en": "As the ancestress of all things, Vud-Ava was considered the oldest goddess and the embodiment of wisdom."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Dalam mitologi Komi, Lyuzimer adalah roh pengganggu yang menguasai pusaran udara dan air. Namanya berasal dari kata yang berarti menghamburkan sesuatu. Pada musim dingin ia menerbangkan salju di permukaan tanah, sedangkan pada musim panas dan gugur ia menghamburkan jerami dari tumpukannya. Tembakau dianggap penangkalnya: begitu kata itu diucapkan, ia berhenti bersenang-senang di padang rumput.",
      "en": "In Komi mythology Lyuzimer is a harmful spirit who rules whirling currents of air and water. His name comes from a word meaning to scatter something.     "id": "Shuvgey adalah sebutan orang Komi untuk kekuatan najis yang mewujud sebagai angin puyuh, menculik orang dan membawa penyakit.",
    "en": "Shuvgey is the Komi name for unclean forces that materialise as a whirlwind, abduct people and bring disease.",
    "claim_ids": [

      "wd-q4527303-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bagi orang Komi-Zyryan dan Komi-Permyak, Shuvgey adalah sebutan kolektif untuk kekuatan najis yang mewujud sebagai angin kencang atau angin puyuh. Dalam wujud angin puyuh ia bisa membawa pergi bahkan lelaki dewasa. Kapak menjadi penangkal terbaik, dan orang percaya bahwa menebas jejak orang yang hilang akan membuat Shuvgey melepaskannya.",
      "en": "For the Komi-Zyryans and Komi-Permyaks, Shuvgey is the collective name for unclean forces that materialise as a strong wind or whirlwind. As a whirlwind it could carry off even a grown man. An axe was the best protection, and people believed that chopping through a missing person's track would make Shuvgey release him.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q4527303-c01",
        "wd-q4527303-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Anak yang diculik melihat lingkaran tarian anak-anak yang memanggilnya, dan bila ia menyambut uluran tangan mereka, ia selamanya dikuasai Shuvgey. Korban bisa kembali jika tetap tenang dan berdoa; benang sutra di pinggang dianggap jimat terbaik. Shuvgey juga dianggap membawa penyakit, termasuk gagap dan epilepsi.",
      "en": "An abducted child sees a ring dance of children calling to him, and if he takes their hands he is in Shuvgey's power forever. Victims could return if they stayed calm and prayed; a silk thread round the waist was thought the best amulet. Shuvgey was also believed to send disease, including stuttering and epilepsy.",

        "wd-q4527303-c03",
        "wd-q4527303-c04",
        "wd-q4527303-c05"
      ]
    },
    {

Menurut orang Komi-Zyryan, badai saat hujan lebat adalah sekaratnya ketika dihukum dewa Yen dengan guntur dan petir.       "id": "wd-q4527303-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q4527303-s1",
      "quote": "собирательное название нечистой силы у коми-зырян и коми-пермяков. Материализуется в виде сильного ветра, вихря.",
